# Integrating with Webflow (/integrations/integrating-with-webflow)



<Callout>
  If you have ideas, feature requests, or bug reports in regards to Webflow integration please send your feedback to us at [help@spline.design](mailto:help@spline.design).
</Callout>

Add interactive 3D content to your websites without the need to write code with the Webflow Integration called Spline Embed.

You can control many of the interactions of your Spline scene directly within Webflow. This allows the use of Webflow Events (e.g.: click, hover, mouse position, scroll) to manipulate the position, rotation, and scale of the available Spline objects.

To learn more about how to use it follow the steps and tips in this doc.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b831f709-2b09-40bc-b51e-4c125ac54e76.mp4" />

## Exporting from Spline [#exporting-from-spline]

1. Open a Spline Scene.
2. Click on “**Export**” in the top toolbar.
3. Select “**Spline Viewer**”
4. Copy the scene link at the top. It should look like this: `https://prod.spline.design/HqdfCmOueigtautT/scene.splinecode `→ feel free to grab this scene to test embedding Webflow.

## Adding Spline Scene in Webflow [#adding-spline-scene-in-webflow]

Inside the Webflow editor, follow these steps to add a Spline Scene:

1. Click the “+” icon on the left toolbar
2. Under “Elements” search for the “Media” section
3. Click on “Spline Scene”
4. There is a default Spline Scene included in the “URL” input field. Paste your own Spline URL (see previous step [Exporting from Spline](#exporting-from-spline)).
5. Click on “Show All Settings” to get access to all the Webflow settings.

To learn more about how to style and size the Spline Scene on your website, check the following docs from Webflow:

* [Style Panel Overview in Webflow](https://university.webflow.com/lesson/style-panel-overview)
* [Element Settings Panel in Webflow](https://webflow.com/glossary/element-settings-panel)

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1045ce55-0945-42c9-84a0-f69ef4b877a6.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/bd73b283-cca5-45c3-87a4-e7818bc18065.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

## Controlling the Spline scene from Webflow [#controlling-the-spline-scene-from-webflow]

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/bdf9c4e1-1a29-41a3-af96-51a22b76721f.mp4" />

1. Select “⚡️ – Interactions” in the right sidebar top corner.
2. Create a new trigger (Element trigger or Page trigger), for this exercise select “**Mouse move in viewport*&#x2A;” with the “&#x2A;*+**” icon next to “**Page trigger**”.
3. Select the trigger.
4. Add an animation (”+” icon next to Mouse Animations).
5. Open the animation with the “⚙️ – cogwheel icon”.
6. At Mouse X Action click on the “+” icon and select “Spline” at the “Integrations” section → This unlocks access to Spline objects.
7. At 0% select the “Spline” object.
8. At the bottom of the sidebar next to “object” select from the dropdown the object you would like to manipulate → “Geometry” in the case of the example in the video.
9. Set the “Geometry” initial position, scale, and rotation, you need to define a value in order to take effect (default values will be ignored from the animation)
10. Now select the Spline object at 100% and there you should be defined by how much should be the object’s position, rotation, and scale be transformed.
11. Now preview the experience with the “👁️ – preview icon” in the top toolbar.
12. Done! Moving the mouse over the webpage should animate the Spline object.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/4ba0e411-7f5b-474e-b6e7-420165bbcf73.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

## Previewing Spline scene in Webflow [#previewing-spline-scene-in-webflow]

In Webflow following options are available to preview your Spline Scene interactions

* **Option 1** → Click on the “👁️ – Toggle Preview” icon in the top toolbar
* **Option 2** → Publish the site in Webflow and load the page to see the Spline scene in action.

## Update Spline scene in Webflow [#update-spline-scene-in-webflow]

#### 1. In Spline [#1-in-spline]

1. Make an update in the Scene (e.g.: change the color of an object),
2. Click on Export
3. Select the Spline Viewer tab
4. Click on Update → Wait until the updates get pushed

#### 2. In Webflow [#2-in-webflow]

<Callout>
  In the case of an already published Webflow project, just reload the website that contains the Spline Embed and your most recent Spline update will be visible.
</Callout>

To see the update in the Webflow Editor follow these steps:

1. Select the Spline Scene.
2. In the right sidebar select “Element Settings”
3. At the “Spline Scene Settings” section click on “Reload Spline Scene”
4. Done, the updates are live!

## Best practices [#best-practices]

* **Scene optimization** → Learn to optimize your scene to make sure your scene loads quickly and runs efficiently on all sorts of devices. [How to optimize your scene](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/how-to-optimize-your-scene.md)
* **Various screen sizes** → To match your scene with responsive behaviours use the Screen Resize Event and match it with the breakpoints from your Webflow environment. [Screen Resize Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/screen-resize-event.md)

# Code Tab (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/code-tab)

> Write code inside a Spline file — the Code tab holds an HTML document layered over the 3D canvas, with a spline API for driving the scene.



The **Code tab** is where a Spline file's own code lives. A file can carry one
**HTML document** — markup, CSS and JavaScript — that the editor renders in a
sandboxed iframe layered exactly over the 3D canvas. Anything you build there
sits on top of the live scene, and an injected `spline` API lets it read and
drive that scene.

It covers two jobs:

* **Web UI over the scene** — HUDs, dashboards, panels, forms and controls.
* **Behavior and motion code** — animation loops, interaction logic, game
  loops. A document can be *headless*: one `<script>` and nothing visible.

The document is part of the file. It saves with it, it's undoable, it syncs to
collaborators, and it ships with your exports.

<Callout type="info">
  This is different from the [Code API for Web](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/web/code-api-for-web.md),
  which drives an **already-published** scene from your own site. The Code tab
  lives *inside* the Spline file and travels with it.
</Callout>

***

## Where to find it [#where-to-find-it]

The Code tab is one of three **Preview | Edit | Code** tabs at the top of the
editor, which switch what the stage shows:

| Tab         | What it shows                                                             |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Preview** | The scene running with your HTML composited over it — what a visitor sees |
| **Edit**    | The bare 3D scene, nothing executing, full editing                        |
| **Code**    | The HTML document's source, over the scene                                |

Open **Code** to write or edit the document. A **Console** strip captures your
`console.*` output and any errors, so `console.log` is the way to debug.

***

## The document [#the-document]

One complete HTML document per file. Your CSS and JavaScript go inline, in the
document itself.

**External libraries work.** `<script src="…">` and remote stylesheets load
normally, so reach for a chart or animation library when it genuinely helps.
Prefer inline code for small things: every remote script is one more thing that
can fail to load at export time.

**Don't rebuild the scene in HTML.** The 3D content is behind you and stays
Spline's job — no WebGL canvases of your own.

### Two layout rules [#two-layout-rules]

1. **Keep the page background transparent.** Don't set a background on `html`
   or `body` — the 3D scene shows through everywhere you don't paint. Style
   your own panels and cards instead.
2. **Pointer events pass through the background.** Clicks on `html`/`body`
   reach the 3D viewport, so orbiting and scene interactions keep working
   between your UI elements. Events on your own elements stay yours — keep
   interactive HTML compact so the scene behind it stays reachable.

Position UI with `fixed` or `absolute` CSS against the full viewport — the
frame covers the canvas exactly.

***

## Talking to the scene [#talking-to-the-scene]

A `spline` object is injected into the document before your scripts run. It is
the seam between your code and the file's 3D content, and it works in both
directions:

* **Read the scene** — variables, the objects in the file and their current
  transforms, and a mesh's live geometry.
* **Drive the scene** — move, hide and restyle objects, change geometry
  parameters, and write variables.
* **Create at runtime** — copy a template object, clone an existing one, or
  build new primitives, text and lights from code.
* **React to what happens** — listen for variable changes and for the events
  you authored on objects, or trigger those events yourself.
* **Go beyond the layer system** — supply custom three.js materials for looks
  Spline's material layers can't express.

<Callout>
  Everything your code changes is **transient**: it's runtime state, never saved
  to the file and never added to undo history, and it resets on reload. That's
  what you want for interactive controls — but if something should permanently
  change the file, edit it in the scene instead.
</Callout>

***

## Edit mode vs Play mode [#edit-mode-vs-play-mode]

* Reads, object updates and variable writes work **while editing**, so you can
  build against the scene in place.
* Events you authored on objects only fire in **Play mode** — the interaction
  system is off while editing, and trying to trigger one warns in the console
  instead.

***

## What ships with exports [#what-ships-with-exports]

The HTML travels with the scene automatically:

| Export                                                                                | HTML content                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| [Public URL](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/web/exporting-as-public-ur-ls.md)                     | ✅                                     |
| [`<spline-viewer>` embed](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/web/exporting-as-spline-viewer.md)       | ✅                                     |
| [Code export](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/web/exporting-as-code.md) running the Spline runtime | ✅                                     |
| three.js / react-three-fiber code export                                              | ❌ — these don't use the Spline engine |

Exports always run in play mode, so the full event system is live there.

<Callout type="warn">
  Custom three.js materials work in the editor and in web exports, but are **not
  supported in `<spline-viewer>` embeds**. If you use them, fall back to a
  layered material so the scene still reads everywhere.
</Callout>

***

## Let the agent write it [#let-the-agent-write-it]

You don't have to write the document by hand. The
[AI Agent](https://docs.spline.design/spline-ai/ai-agent.md) can write and edit the document for you —
describe the UI or behavior you want and it produces the markup and the
`spline` calls, leaving source you can edit in the Code tab.


# Directional Light (/lighting/directional-light)



Directional lights are ideal to simulate illumination from the sun or in open scenes.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/63102c57-9c79-4306-b2f5-bd6468c4e789.png" alt="image" />

### How does it work? [#how-does-it-work]

Directional lights are based on the directional angle between the position of the light and the origin of the coordinate system. The line traced between these two points defines the direction.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ff2cb51f-2165-4b84-98e5-5d23cc1f37ec.mp4" />


# Environment & HDRi (/lighting/environment-and-hdri)

> Light a scene with an HDRi image or a physical sky — image-based lighting, reflections, and the background your scene sits in.



Lights aren't the only way to illuminate a scene. The **Sky** gives your scene
an *environment*: a surrounding image that lights every surface from all
directions at once and shows up in reflections. It's what makes materials —
especially metals and glossy surfaces — read as if they're somewhere real.

You'll find it in the scene's page settings, in the **Sky** panel, with a toggle
to turn it on. There are two modes:

| Mode         | What it is                                                                                           |
| ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **HDRi**     | An equirectangular environment image — a studio, an interior, a landscape — wrapped around the scene |
| **Physical** | A simulated sky and atmosphere, with a sun position and weather                                      |

***

## HDRi [#hdri]

Pick an **Image** from the built-in presets — neutral and colored studios,
interiors, landscapes, gradients — or upload your own. Alongside regular image
files, `.hdr` and `.exr` files are accepted, which is what you'll get from an
HDRi library.

| Control             | What it does                                                                                                            |
| ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Image**           | The environment image, from the presets or your own upload                                                              |
| **Rotation**        | Spins the environment around the scene, from 0° to 360° — use it to move highlights and reflections where you want them |
| **Blur**            | Softens the environment. Sharp environments give crisp reflections; blurred ones give smooth, diffuse light             |
| **Show BG**         | Whether the image is drawn *behind* the scene, or only used to light it                                                 |
| **Affect Lighting** | How much the environment drives the scene's lighting — see below                                                        |
| **Light Intensity** | Multiplier on that lighting                                                                                             |

<Callout type="idea">
  Turning **Show BG** off is the classic product-render setup: the environment
  still lights the scene and appears in reflections, but your flat background
  color shows behind the objects instead of a photographic skybox.
</Callout>

Presets you pick are stored in the file, so a scene stays self-contained when
you share it or hand it off.

***

## Physical [#physical]

Instead of an image, the Physical mode simulates a sky. Set the **Time** of day
to place the sun, choose a **Weather** preset from clear through to storm, and
the atmosphere and light color follow.

| Control                                  | What it does                                              |
| ---------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Time**                                 | Time of day, which drives the sun's position              |
| **Weather**                              | Cloud-cover preset, from clear to overcast and storm      |
| **Clouds**                               | Turns the cloud layers on and off                         |
| **Wind** and **Animate Wind**            | How fast clouds drift, and whether they move in real time |
| **Weather Seed**                         | Regenerates the cloud pattern                             |
| **Exposure**                             | Overall brightness of the sky                             |
| **Affect Lighting**, **Light Intensity** | Same as HDRi — how strongly the sky lights the scene      |

***

## How environment lighting works [#how-environment-lighting-works]

With **Affect Lighting** on, the environment becomes a light source: the scene
gets a dome of light taking its color from the environment, plus a directional
light following the sun. While that's on, the scene's own **Ambient** light
steps aside — the environment dome replaces it — and comes back when you turn it
off.

The setting has three positions:

* **Off** — the environment is only a backdrop and reflections; your own lights
  do the lighting.
* **Low** — full sky-driven light, but environment reflections are limited to
  the surfaces where they're most visible, like metals and glossy materials. A
  performance dial for heavy scenes.
* **Full** — every physical material samples the environment.

<Callout>
  The Physical sky renders on the **WebGPU** pipeline. Check your scene's
  **Renderer** setting before publishing — see
  [Play Settings](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/play-settings.md).
</Callout>

***

## Getting the most out of it [#getting-the-most-out-of-it]

* **Environments and PBR materials go together.** Roughness, metalness and
  reflections have nothing to reflect without an environment, which is why a
  metal object can look flat and grey in an unlit scene. See
  [PBR Materials](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/basics/pbr-materials.md).
* **Rotate before you add lights.** Often an environment rotation gets you the
  highlight you were about to add a light for.
* **Blur for mood, sharp for product.** A blurred environment reads as soft
  studio light; a sharp one puts recognizable detail in your reflections.
* **Combine with your own lights.** The environment handles ambient light and
  reflections; a [Directional Light](https://docs.spline.design/lighting/directional-light.md) or
  [Spot Light](https://docs.spline.design/lighting/spot-light.md) still gives you control over shadows and
  accents.

[Working with Lights](https://docs.spline.design/lighting/working-with-lights.md),
[Reflection Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/reflection-layer.md),
[Working with Fog](https://docs.spline.design/cameras/working-with-fog.md)


# Point Light (/lighting/point-light)



Point lights – as the name implies – emit light from a point in the space and fade away with the distance. These lights can be useful to represent the emission of light from a great variety of lights in the world, like house lamps, traffic lights, car lights, and more.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b811e46f-85c4-4611-8e8a-4a2fe8683585.png" alt="image" />

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/61d27743-f8bc-4076-a476-d97ad7825842.mp4" />


# Soft Shadows (/lighting/soft-shadows)



<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/z5q_CcaS0I4" title="YouTube video player" />

You can achieve more realistic shadows in your 3D scenes by controlling the Blur and Penumbra values on your light parameters.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5665c8f8-3c1d-4b60-9ebc-2afc0084137f.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/4b6aed44-4ca5-4d13-80da-82949a989404.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

1. Go to Environment Settings and on the Shadows property select “Soft”.
2. The “Detail” property is set to Low by default which means that it’s faster but also more noisy. You can change it to Normal or High to get smoother results.
3. Then select your light and you’ll notice that some new parameters are now available for Directional and Spot Lights:
   * **Blur** - Controls the general blurriness of the shadow.
   * **Penumbra** - Controls the blurriness across the distance of the shadow.


# Spot Light (/lighting/spot-light)



Spot lights are coned-shape lights that are usually ideal to simulate artificial lighting from objects like lamps or street lights.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d1d1e60c-c396-41f5-98f5-d93bf36733ae.png" alt="image" />

### How does the Spot Light Work? [#how-does-the-spot-light-work]

Just like the Directional Light, the spot light works based on the directional angle of the light.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/c6021f07-0c42-4ba4-afc7-d0ca72aa57f2.mp4" />


# Working with Lights (/lighting/working-with-lights)



On Spline, you can create different types of lights to generate different types of illumination for your scene.

To create a light, press the "+" icon on the Toolbar and choose between one of the available lights:

* Directional Light
* Point Light
* Spot Light

### Lights Properties [#lights-properties]

Each light contains both shared and unique properties.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/283af496-c360-41e1-a87c-4839bf661f10.mp4" />

### Default Lighting [#default-lighting]

By default, any new scene on Spline already includes a Directional Light and an Ambient Light that sets the default lighting for any object.

The properties for the Ambient Light can be found on the Environment panel on the right sidebar.

While this works fairly well for basic scenes, it may be something that you want to toggle off or adjust depending on your visual goals for your scene.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9ab27c0d-b042-4cf5-8199-05f54d923773.mp4" />


# Comments & Feedback in 3D (/sharing-collaboration-and-workspaces/comments-feedback-in-3-d)



Comments allow a more intuitive collaboration workflow with your team and friends right inside Spline. Leave feedback, save notes, or share ideas by simply leaving a comment on your 3D scenes.

## Creating a Comment [#creating-a-comment]

To create a new comment thread:

1. Select the comment tool from the toolbar or use the shortcut **C**
2. On the viewport, press on the position where you want to create a new comment
3. Input your message and press **Enter** or the send button

<Callout>
  Comments will stick to objects in the 3D space.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9aa4a7fa-8af2-4d60-afd1-739a6b634a0e.mp4" />

## Creating a Reply [#creating-a-reply]

To reply to an existing comment thread:

1. Open the comment thread by pressing the comment pin on the viewport
2. Type your reply on the input at the bottom and press **Enter** or the send button

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f4a12e67-7a4a-40a9-83a8-4e1f6c189a41.mp4" />

## Editing a Comment or Reply [#editing-a-comment-or-reply]

After identifying the comment or reply you want to edit:

1. Open the comment thread by pressing the comment pin on the viewport
2. Press the options icon to the right of the commenter's name and select ‘Edit’
3. Make changes to the original comment/reply and press **Enter** or the save button

<Callout>
  Both Editors and Viewers can only edit comments or replies made by themselves.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/dca71488-de34-4eca-89d7-1be5a5bad44d.mp4" />

## Deleting a Comment or Reply [#deleting-a-comment-or-reply]

After identifying the comment or reply you want to delete:

1. Open the comment thread by pressing the comment pin on the viewport
2. Press the options icon to the right of the commenter's name and choose Edit
3. On the confirmation modal, press Delete.

<Callout>
  Editors can delete/resolve comments or replies from everyone while viewers can only delete their own comments and replies.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/239e20bc-86f9-4cb6-a777-28388b950d9d.mp4" />


# Community Platform (/sharing-collaboration-and-workspaces/community-platform)



The Community Platform is a place for Spline designers to publish their 3d designs, find inspiration, and learn from others by remixing scenes.

* Discover and follow other creators
* Build your profile
* Like and comment on scenes
* Showcase your 3D scenes

***

## Understanding the Community Platform [#understanding-the-community-platform]

You can find below the different aspects of the Community platform and know how you can interact and engage with the community.

### Explore Page [#explore-page]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f9628988-fecb-4a6b-aa43-7988dad3758e.png" alt="image" />

1. Navigate between explore, feed, and notifications tabs in addition to searching the community.
2. Press the publish button to contribute your 3d designs to the community.
3. Explore Spline designs and creators, and follow them to see a curated feed.
4. Switch between trending, popular, and new designs to see filtered views.

### Feed Page [#feed-page]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/c89f731d-4a0a-4fee-a4d0-0e8461f00b8d.png" alt="image" />

1. Share your thoughts along with a design to inspire the community.
2. Follow recommended creators to get a curated feed and updates.
3. Engage with the community by reposting, commenting, and remixing the designs.

### Community File [#community-file]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3470f450-4227-4590-a9f0-a41aa3830bdf.png" alt="image" />

   * Repost, Like, or remix the design to engage with it.
2. Support creators by sharing the design with your team or socials.
3. Share your thoughts, kudos, or feedback by commenting on the design.

***

## Publishing scenes to the Community [#publishing-scenes-to-the-community]

You can contribute your designs to the community to build your profile and help others.

There are three ways to publish your designs to the community.

### Publish from Community [#publish-from-community]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/bd43e409-f0d3-4f43-9abd-613c676f0a50.png" alt="image" />

1. Press on the community tab from the navigator on the left-hand side.
2. Click the “Publish” button to upload your designs to the community.
3. Continue the process shown after clicking the button to post your designs on the Community.

### Publish from Feed [#publish-from-feed]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f2c8bbca-a4c5-49de-9c07-3633487d5801.png" alt="image" />

1. Press on the feed tab from the community page.
2. Locate the post section in the feed section.
3. Select the design, write a description/thought, and share by clicking on “Publish”.

### Publish from Editor [#publish-from-editor]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9a14e23b-08a6-46ee-a975-523bd4147e4f.png" alt="image" />

2. Switch to the “Publish” tab to share it with the community.
3. Press continue and complete the next steps to upload the design to the community.


# File Sharing (/sharing-collaboration-and-workspaces/file-sharing)



Spline is a collaborative tool, meaning you can invite other people and work together on the same file in real-time. Invitations use the account email so everyone needs to be signed up.

## Sharing a File [#sharing-a-file]

To invite someone to one of your files you'll need the account email you're sharing your file with.

1. Open the context menu from a file (press the right mouse button) and press `Share`.
2. Add the email of the account you're sharing your file and select the role of the invitee, whether is viewer (can access and view the file) or editor (full access to the file).
3. Press `Invite` to send the invitation.
4. Alternatively, you can copy and share the link to the file. You can select if anyone with the link is a viewer, editor, or you can make the link private so no one can access the file from it.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d108e96b-dfb0-4a23-87b4-c833e6d13230.mp4" />

## Accepting a File Invitation [#accepting-a-file-invitation]

If you're on the receiving end of an invitation, make sure to check your ‘Inbox’ tab. A new notification will show up, letting you know someone invited you to join a file.

From there you can choose if you'd like to accept or decline the invitation:

* If accepted, you’ll automatically be redirected to the file link. To access the file again at a later time, you’ll find it on the ‘Shared with me’ tab.
* If declined, the notification disappears, so you can keep your Inbox tab clean.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d839a724-65d6-4f43-b3fa-5ac19ee30a9a.mp4" />


# Files View (/sharing-collaboration-and-workspaces/files-view)



The ‘My Files’ tab is the place where you can organize all your personal files.

Use the file previews to quickly find what you want to work on and rest assured that your file will be always saved and updated with our autosave feature.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9ccdac22-f5cc-4966-a8b8-e0fff72b6827.png" alt="image" />

## Creating a New File [#creating-a-new-file]

There are a few ways to create a new file:

1. The ‘New File’ button on the top right of your screen.
2. Opening a context menu anywhere on the empty space of your files view and choosing ‘New File’.
3. On the desktop app, press the ‘+’ icon on the top where the file tabs live.

## Organizing your Files [#organizing-your-files]

Through the file context menu, you can do most actions. You can make a &#x2A;*new folder from selection,** &#x2A;*Share Copy Link,** **Duplicate**, &#x2A;*Rename, Move,** and &#x2A;*Delete a file.**

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b10dba80-f756-4a13-b588-0f2e36cab357.mp4" />

## Selecting Files [#selecting-files]

Selecting your files is supposed to work in a familiar way from other tools or even from your own operating system. See below some of the ways to do it.

**Single Click** - Select a single file

**CMD + Click** - Select multiple files 1 by 1

**Shift + Click** - Select all files between the first and last selected

**Box Selection** - Draw a box selection around the files you want to select

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8042cf02-ea10-4fcb-a729-ecf8f93823f9.mp4" />

## Folders [#folders]

With one of our paid plans, you can now create folders to keep all your files organized.

There are a few ways to create a new folder:

1. The ‘New Folder’ button on the top of the page.
2. Opening a context menu anywhere on the empty space of your files view and choosing ‘New Folder’.
3. Selecting multiple files, opening a context menu, and selecting ‘New folder from selection’.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5d193aa4-c7d4-4a2b-a649-8626121b9b04.mp4" />

You can create folder structures as deep as you need them to be. The breadcrumb on top of the page should help you navigate in the folders.

To move files into folders or out of them you can easily drag and drop your files.

To drag multiple files first select them and then start dragging from one of them.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/035f2b2d-e9c9-4d24-93e4-bcf92ab74ed8.mp4" />


# Real-time Collaboration in 3D (/sharing-collaboration-and-workspaces/real-time-collaboration-in-3-d)



Historically the primary way of creating in 3D has been as single users building individual files and manually sharing these back and forth with the people we’re working with.

With real-time collaboration, you can now work together with others on the same file and see the changes happen straight away. You won’t wonder anymore if you’re working on the correct file with the latest changes since all files are now autosaved.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/51a3b97d-3f2a-4d7d-9155-0fedc61285b9.mp4" />

On the editor, you can see who’s currently inside the file by looking at the active users panel on the top right of your screen.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/0b9a202a-7099-4926-a1b8-0a16fb549be6.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

By clicking the Share button, the sharing modal will come up, where you can invite other people to join your file.

Control link permissions:

* Anyone with this link (Viewer)
* Anyone with this link (Editor)
* Only people invited can open this link.

Choose from following permissions for invited individuals:

* Viewer → view only access.
* Editor → edit access to file.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/82a7e963-4307-42b4-8996-0db22ae342e3.png" alt="image" />

On the canvas, the pointers and labels of other users will let you know the position from where they’re currently approaching the scene from.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1d8e06af-322e-4ce3-91b5-605e7ec6c8cd.mp4" />


# Team Libraries (/sharing-collaboration-and-workspaces/team-libraries)



A Team Library is your central hub for design assets. Create a library with components, materials, colors, images, and audio assets for your team to use. Easily cross-reference and update them across files, without the hassle of individual updates.

Simplify collaboration and establish an efficient design system with Team Libraries. Discover how to integrate it into your workflow with the following steps.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/33fb29ac-2cec-4109-a7d2-56491519bd07.mp4" />

<Callout>
  Team Libraries are available on the Enterprise plan. [Contact Sales
  ➔](https://spline.design/enterprise)
</Callout>

## Create a Team Library [#create-a-team-library]

1. Create a new file or open an existing one. The file should be created or stored on a team as libraries are only available for teams.
2. Create assets in the file (components, colors, images, media, audio).
3. Press the “Assets” tab on the left sidebar.
4. Press the “Manage Libraries” button.
5. Press the “Use as Team Library” button and the publishing will start.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/337fc9e0-5bc3-40a0-8ddf-6d41b84e9c45.mp4" />

<Callout>
  It's a good practice to create a dedicated file for your Team Library, such as your 3D design
  system, which you can reference in multiple files and make updates to.
</Callout>

## Disable a Team Library [#disable-a-team-library]

1. Press the “Assets” tab on the left sidebar.
2. Press the “Manage Libraries” button.
3. Hover the “Enabled” element next to the library name.
4. Press the “Disable Library” button.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9584d4c5-f6a9-4fae-ae00-74ccc78608b0.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Callout>
  The assets on this file will not be visible in other team files and the connection will be broken
  with assets already used in other files. Enabling the library again will reconnect the assets used
  in other files.
</Callout>

## Enable Team Libraries on other files [#enable-team-libraries-on-other-files]

1. Open the file where you would like to use and reference a Team Library.
2. Press the “Assets” tab on the left sidebar.
3. Press the “Manage Libraries” button.
4. At the “Team Libraries” section, turn on the libraries you would like to have access to on your file.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f2da90de-40a4-4296-83f5-cfcb4a339088.mp4" />

## Use Team Libraries’ assets on other files [#use-team-libraries-assets-on-other-files]

1. After enabling a Team Library, on the “Assets” tab, you can select the specific library from the dropdown menu and start using components or assets from that library.
2. Drag and drop components or materials from the asset browser.
3. When an object is selected, get access to the enabled libraries directly in the assets browsers and swap them (component, material, color, image, audio, or media).

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ee1db6e7-8e3c-45ee-9820-226e1d75aa70.mp4" />

## Push new changes on a Library [#push-new-changes-on-a-library]

1. Open the Team Library file.
2. Make any changes to your assets (components, material, color, image, media, or audio).
3. Press the “Assets” tab on the left sidebar.
4. Press the "Push New Changes" button that will show up on the sidebar or inside the Library Manager.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/33f63111-537d-40d3-a518-09af589f0b14.mp4" />

## Get new changes from Libraries [#get-new-changes-from-libraries]

1. Open the file where you would like to get the changes from the team library.
2. A purple dot will show up on the assets tab, letting you know there are new updates from your libraries.
3. Press the “Assets” tab on the left sidebar.
4. Press the “Get New Changes” button to get the new updates.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/96bf8628-0771-4a25-9693-8629fcb74789.mp4" />


# Workspaces (/sharing-collaboration-and-workspaces/workspaces)



Collaborate with your whole team knowing that your files are saved in a shared space and always accessible to any member.

## Creating a new workspace [#creating-a-new-workspace]

To create a new workspace, open the workspaces dropdown on the top left and click the ‘Create Workspace‘ button. You’ll be prompted to give it a name, invite other members, and select a plan.

## Creating a new project [#creating-a-new-project]

On workspaces, you can create projects to organize your team files by project, status, type, or anything that suits your team needs.

To create a new project, you can press the ‘+’ icon next to projects on the left sidebar.

When you have no projects:

* Press the "+ New Project" button on the left sidebar.
* Visit the projects view, then press the "+ New Project" button.

## Changing between workspaces [#changing-between-workspaces]

On the top left of your dashboard, you'll see the name of the workspace you're currently in.

To change to another workspace, simply click on the workspace name to open the workspace dropdown and select a different workspace to open it.

Once changed, you'll see all files related to that workspace. Including the "My Files" tab, which are your private files on each workspace.

## Workspace Settings [#workspace-settings]

You can go into your workspace settings by opening the workspaces dropdown, and pressing Settings. You can also quickly navigate to the settings of a different workspace by clicking the gear icon next to the workspace name.

There you’ll be able to change workspace name, view and manage workspace members and billing.

## Managing workspace permissions [#managing-workspace-permissions]

To invite members to your workspace there are multiple ways:

* From the workspace dropdown, pressing the invite button
* On the projects view, pressing the "Invite Members" button on the top right
* By going into Settings > Members, and pressing the "Invite Members" button

From the Members list, you can manage individual permissions or remove members.

The permissions available are:

* Owner - The member who created the workspace
* Editor - A member with full permissions to create, edit files
* Viewer - A member who can view all workspace files and view files inside the editor.

## Spline for Enterprise [#spline-for-enterprise]

In case you need &#x2A;**Advanced Permission Controls**&#x2A;, &#x2A;**Multi-team Organization**&#x2A; and other features like for &#x2A;**SAML Single Sign-On**&#x2A;, &#x2A;**SOC 2 Compliance**&#x2A;, &#x2A;**Priority Support**&#x2A;, &#x2A;**Enterprise-grade Security***, we recommend that you get in contact with our sales team.

[Contact Sales ➔](https://spline.design/enterprise)


# AI Agent (/spline-ai/ai-agent)

> Build and edit 3D scenes by describing what you want. The agent works the editor the same way you do — with real, undoable edits.



The **AI Agent** builds and edits 3D scenes for you. Describe what you want and it
does the work through the editor itself — creating objects, setting up materials
and lighting, animating, wiring interactivity — using the same commands you'd
use by hand.

That last part matters: every edit the agent makes is a **normal editor edit**.
It lands in your undo history, it syncs to anyone collaborating in the file, and
you can take over at any point.

<Callout type="tip">
  Designing in Hana instead? It has its own assistant — see
  [AI Agent in Hana](https://docs.spline.design/hana-a-canvas-for-interactivity/ai-in-hana.md).
</Callout>

<Callout>
  The agent runs on your workspace's monthly **AI credits**, included with every
  paid plan. See [AI credits](https://docs.spline.design/account/pricing.md#ai-credits).
</Callout>

***

## Where to find it [#where-to-find-it]

Open the **Agent** tab at the top of the left sidebar — the first of the three
tabs, next to **Objects** and **Assets**. It's the tab the editor opens on, so
it's usually already there.

Type what you want in the prompt box at the bottom of the panel and press Enter.

### The panel, top to bottom [#the-panel-top-to-bottom]

| Where                | What it is                                                                                                              |
| -------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Sidebar tab row      | **Agent**, plus **New chat** and **Chat history** buttons on the right                                                  |
| Middle of the panel  | The conversation                                                                                                        |
| Above the prompt box | Queued prompts, your selection chips, and any attachments                                                               |
| The prompt box       | Type here. `/` opens the command menu                                                                                   |
| Bottom row           | **+** to attach, the **model** and **effort** pickers, and **Send** — which becomes **Stop** while the agent is working |

***

## Writing a good prompt [#writing-a-good-prompt]

The agent reads your scene before it acts, so you can talk about what's already
there rather than describing everything from scratch.

* **Say what you want, not how to build it.** "A cabin on a rocky island at
  sunset" gets further than a list of primitives and coordinates.
* **Select first, then refer to the selection.** Whatever you have selected when
  you hit send becomes the meaning of "this", "it" and "these", and it scopes an
  edit that doesn't name a target. The objects you selected appear as chips above
  the prompt so you can see exactly what the agent will act on.
* **Iterate.** "Make the roof steeper", "add fog", "warmer light" — follow-ups
  are usually faster and more predictable than one enormous prompt.
* **Ask it to fix what you can see.** It looks at the result, so "the door is
  floating, put it on the ground" works.

### Attaching images and files [#attaching-images-and-files]

The &#x2A;*+** button at the bottom-left of the prompt box attaches an **image**,
**media** or a **document** — useful for reference art, a style you want matched,
or a model to work from. Attachments appear above the prompt box before you send;
click one to view it full size, or use the ✕ to remove it.

### Selection chips [#selection-chips]

Select objects in the viewport or the **Objects** tab before you send, and they
appear as chips just above the prompt box. The chips name the first object and
count the rest (`+3 more`) — hover either part for the full list.

They stay on the message after it's sent, so scrolling back through a conversation
still shows what "make these blue" was pointing at, even if those objects were
later renamed or deleted.

***

## What the agent can do [#what-the-agent-can-do]

Beyond building and editing the scene, you can ask it to:

* **Check and fix its own work** — "the door is floating, put it on the ground"
  works, because it can see the result.
* **Generate images** — textures, reference art, or image content for the scene.
* **Generate 3D models** — from text or an image, inserted straight into the
  scene. See [AI 3D Generation](https://docs.spline.design/generate/ai-3d-generation.md).
* **Speed up a slow scene** — ask it to optimise, or send it a fix from the
  Performance panel. See
  [How to optimize your scene](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/how-to-optimize-your-scene.md).
* **Write code over the canvas** — HUDs, interfaces and game logic that drive the
  3D scene, in the **Preview / Edit / Code** tabs at the top of the editor.

### Slash commands [#slash-commands]

Type `/` in the prompt box to pin a request to one job instead of leaving the
choice to the agent:

| Command  | What it does                                    |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| `/image` | Generate an AI image from the text that follows |
| `/3d`    | Generate a 3D model from the text that follows  |

***

## Models and effort [#models-and-effort]

Both pickers sit on the bottom row of the panel, to the right of the &#x2A;*+** button.

The first sets which model handles your request. Tiers run
from **Fast** — quickest, least detail — up to **Ultra Max** and **Astra**, the
highest quality and the most expensive. Each option shows what it's good for, so
you can pick against the job rather than memorising the list.

The second, **effort**, sets how much the model thinks before acting, from
**Minimal** through **Max**. Higher effort helps with scenes that have a lot of
moving parts — interactivity, physics, precise layout — and mostly just costs time
on simple ones. Not every model offers every level; the control shows the ones
available for what you've picked.

Higher is not automatically better. For small edits, a quicker tier at moderate
effort usually gets you there sooner and spends fewer credits.

***

## Working while the agent works [#working-while-the-agent-works]

**You don't have to wait.** Press Enter while the agent is working and your prompt
joins a queue. Queued prompts appear as a numbered stack just above the prompt box,
under a **QUEUED** header — the numbers are the order they'll run in.

* **Pause**, in that header, holds the queue so you can see how the first change
  lands before committing to the rest. **Clear**, next to it, empties it.
* **✕** on any row drops just that prompt.
* **Stop** — the button that replaces Send while the agent works — interrupts the
  current run *and* clears the queue. Stopping means stopping, not carrying on
  with whatever was lined up behind it.
* If something fails, the queue stays put and visible instead of running on.

Each queued prompt keeps the text, attachments and selection you had when you
typed it, and stays with its conversation even if you move to another file.

### The viewport follows along [#the-viewport-follows-along]

Your camera moves to keep the work in frame while the agent builds, and settles on
a view of what it made when it finishes.

**Orbiting, panning or zooming stops that.** If you take the camera, you keep it.

***

## Conversations [#conversations]

Chats are saved to your account and kept per file. The two buttons at the
right-hand end of the sidebar's tab row manage them:

* **New chat** starts a fresh conversation in the current file.
* **Chat history** lists your previous conversations in this file — click one to
  switch to it, or use the ✕ on a row to delete it.

Reopening a file drops you back into the conversation you were last in. To copy a
reply out, hover the message and use the copy button.

***

## Credits [#credits]

The agent spends your workspace's monthly **AI credits**. A banner appears above
the prompt box once you've used more than 90% of your monthly allowance, showing
the percentage used; clicking it opens the upgrade options.

See [AI credits](https://docs.spline.design/account/pricing.md#ai-credits) for what each plan includes and how
to add more.

***

## Limitations [#limitations]

* The agent works on **one file at a time**, and on one change at a time — which
  is why extra prompts queue rather than running together.
* It is much better at **intent** than at exact numeric layout. If something needs
  to sit at a precise coordinate, it's usually quicker to place it yourself.
* Complex requests can apply only partly. When that happens the step is marked
  with an amber warning and the agent corrects the rest on its next attempt —
  that's normal, not a failure.
* It won't change a scene while you're in **Play mode**, and it won't repoint a
  camera you've deliberately framed yourself.


# AI Style Transfer (/spline-ai/ai-style-transfer)



<Callout type="warn">
  **AI Style Transfer is now deprecated.**
</Callout>

With AI Style Transfer you can generate images using your 3D scenes as a base.

It’s a great way to quickly visualize your ideas or expand your 3D compositions with extra details.

***

## How to use AI Style Transfer [#how-to-use-ai-style-transfer]

### Accessing AI Style Transfer [#accessing-ai-style-transfer]

The AI Style Transfer feature can be accessed from the toolbar, by pressing the AI button.

### Using AI Style Transfer [#using-ai-style-transfer]

Once you open AI Style Transfer, you’ll find a new inspector sidebar and you’ll also see the result of your first image generation. From there you can start tweaking the settings or changing your scene to achieve other results. Generation happens automatically in most cases, but you can always press the Generate button to generate a new image.

***

## Styles Panel [#styles-panel]

### Seed [#seed]

Each image generation uses a randomly generated seed. You can lock it to maintain the same result and tweak it slightly with prompts or modifiers.

### Prompt [#prompt]

Prompts are used to influence your image generations. In this input, you can type descriptive sentences or words of what you want your final image to look like.

### Style Presets [#style-presets]

These are presets you can use as a base for your image generation.

### Modifiers [#modifiers]

Modifiers are extra keywords you can toggle On to influence your final result.

### Advanced Settings [#advanced-settings]

Here you can find additional settings to get even more precision on your image generations.

* **Exclude** → Sentences or words of things that you don’t want to see in your generation.
* **Precision** → How many steps does the image generation take. The longer the more precise it will be but will also increase processing time.
* **Style** → How much do the selected style and prompts influence the generations.
* **Variance** → How much the generation takes into account the 3D scene.
* **Mode**
  * **Outline** - Based on the outline of your 3D scene.
  * **Edges** - A mix of outline and shadows.
  * **Shapes** - A normal map of your 3D scene

***

## Limitations [#limitations]

The development and research of artificial intelligence (AI) is an ongoing process with several factors that can limit its capabilities. You will likely find bugs and some issues, but we are improving the system every day. Your feedback can help us improve it.


# AI Textures (/spline-ai/ai-textures)



Use Artificial Intelligence to generate textures like **grass**, **concrete**, **wood**, **ceramic**, and **patterns** just by typing!

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/c1dd4786-15d7-4eee-8bbe-eed59415e410.mp4" />

<Callout>
  **AI Texture Generation** is a paid feature. It runs on your workspace's monthly **AI credits**, included with every paid plan — **Hobby**, **Pro** and **Max**. There is no separate AI subscription to buy. See [AI credits](https://docs.spline.design/account/pricing.md#ai-credits) for the monthly allowance on each plan.
</Callout>

***

## How to Generate Textures [#how-to-generate-textures]

To start with AI Texture Generation, you’ll need to:

1. Select an object.
2. Change the layer to **Image** type by clicking on the dropdown icon at the **Materials**.
3. Open the **Image Panel** by clicking on the thumbnail.
4. Click on the **Generate with AI** button in the Image panel.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8da8ea13-8243-431c-8f03-4d950fed902b.png" alt="image" />

## Generate AI Texture Panel [#generate-ai-texture-panel]

On this panel, you’ll be able to generate new textures by writing your text prompts on the input field.

1. Click on the input field &#x2A;*“Type to create anything…”**
2. **Type in some text**. Below the input, you’ll find some suggestions to help you get started and spark some ideas. Feel free to go wild and experiment with the results!
3. **Click on the purple sparkle button** or hit enter to generate and apply the texture.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/12d29b06-8d75-4aa5-baca-67ae562df3e8.png" alt="image" />

Additional features on the panel:

* Download the texture as an image by clicking on the download icon.
* Change the resolution of the texture via the dropdown at the bottom:
  * Small (512 x 512 px)
  * Medium (1024x1024 px)
  * Large (2048 x 2048 px)
* Save it as an image asset by clicking on the “Save as asset” button at the bottom


# Spell AI 3D Worlds (/spline-ai/spell-ai-3d-worlds)



Spell is an experimental AI model made by Spline intended to create entire 3D scenes or “Worlds” from one single image. It empowers you to bring your ideas to life by converting static inputs into immersive 3D worlds.

The worlds are consistent toward the initial image input and are represented in a volume that can be rendered as a Gaussian Splatting.

Spell is currently highly experimental, and it's only meant for early adopters interested in testing the tech or helping our team test the model in its early stages while it's still being trained. Pricing is intentionally high during this phase to avoid high expenses on GPU costs.

[**→ Read the announcement**](https://blog.spline.design/introducing-spell)

<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/PtCKiidsXC4" title="YouTube video player" />

## Creating your first 3D world [#creating-your-first-3d-world]

You can access Spell at [spell.spline.design](http://spell.spline.design) to start creating your first 3D scene is simple — type a text prompt or upload an image.

<Callout>
  **Note:** Spell is at an early stage, with limited access, meaning you'll need to subscribe in order to try it. Currently, Spell pricing is separated from Spline Editor pricing, and the pricing structure will likely change once we decide on the right direction.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/98c9c4a8-bf4c-4827-b1fc-17666466ab0f.mp4" />

#### Text Prompt [#text-prompt]

Describe your idea in words, and you'll get 4 images related to your text prompt.

#### Image Prompt [#image-prompt]

Use an image as your starting point. You'll get your original image plus 3 others, which are variations of the original you uploaded.

#### Generating the 3D Gaussian Splatting Volume [#generating-the-3d-gaussian-splatting-volume]

By pressing on any of the images generated from the text or image prompt, the 3D generation will get started and added to the generation queue. You can also use the "Generate 3D" button when hovering any of the images.

Once the 3D generation is done, you'll get a notification, and you can interact with your 3D scene, tweak interaction settings, and use the public link to embed it anywhere on the web.

<Callout>
  **Note:** Images with well-defined shapes work better. Abstract or overly complex shapes might not produce good results (yet).
</Callout>

## How it works [#how-it-works]

At its core, Spell is a type of diffusion model that can generate 3D worlds with realistic multi-view consistency across a wide range of categories, including people, objects, environments, 3D characters, and more.

The model can render images from multiple angles of a particular subject with high accuracy and detail, as well as generate controlled camera paths, all while staying consistent with the 3D scene.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d77ba384-d26f-4050-bb13-8e3c63ddebd8.mp4" />

## Scene Settings [#scene-settings]

**Public Link** - share your 3D scene and embed it anywhere on the web.

**Background** - control the background of your scene.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/38f52500-02de-42c6-991b-b5497ea8f9b7.png" alt="image" />

### Play Settings [#play-settings]

The play settings affect your scene on the public link and when you go into play mode.

**Auto orbit** - Make your scene orbit automatically like a turntable.

**Interactions**

* **Page Scroll** - Setting Page scroll to Yes will allow the website to be scrolled when your scene is embedded and you hover it. Setting it to No will disable scrolling.
* **Touch Settings** - Decide how interacting with the scene works on touch devices.
* **Orbit** - Enable or Disable the ability to orbit the scene.
  * **Orbit Limits** - Apply limits on the orbit interaction.
* **Pan** - Enable or Disable the ability to pan the scene.
  * **Pan Limits** - Apply limits on the pan interaction.
* **Zoom** - Enable or Disable the ability to zoom the scene.
  * **Zoom Limits** - Apply limits on the zoom interaction.
* **On Hover** - Enable or Disable orbiting when hovering the scene.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/79f92a9f-19f6-4e68-9a6b-cab00c6b698a.png" alt="image" />

## Generation Queue [#generation-queue]

You can access the generation queue in the top right corner. There, you’ll find a list of all your running generations as well as those that have been completed.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/00a439ae-70dd-4a50-b004-5fdbeb5c645b.png" alt="image" />

## Explore [#explore]

On the Explore page, you’ll find a collection of examples and prompts to spark your creativity. It’s a great way to see what’s possible and find inspiration for your own 3D creations.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d5164d1c-ab1d-41da-bc27-e835742f1c57.mp4" />


# 3D Modeling Tools (/designing-in-3-d/modeling/3d-modeling-tools)



The modeling tools enable you to edit geometries to create more complex shapes.

To edit a geometry, press `Smooth & Edit` from the Shape panel in the right sidebar. After this, the object will be now converted into a [subdivision surface](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivision_surface).

You can Extrude, Inset, or generate Edge Loops:

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1bbdb048-c4d3-45d9-bc7f-1d97c8193e53.mp4" />

You can add Loop Cuts and use the Slide Tool to reposition the edges:

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3a5337b5-aead-440a-b6e6-ec9a0b1bfe3b.mp4" />

Also, you can modify and dissolve vertices (points), faces (polygons), or edges (lines):

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1a997608-7b6d-4c3f-ac2b-1349ef985bb1.mp4" />

Finally, you can fill holes by selecting the edges:

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f160af37-7f08-4491-bad3-473cc3a2e987.mp4" />

***

The following parameters can be adjusted under the settings menu:

### Extrude & Inset Mode [#extrude--inset-mode]

Controls if the selected geometry will be extruded or insetted grouped or individually.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ae02867f-e44f-4058-904f-a3319ce00071.mp4" />

### Extrude Direction [#extrude-direction]

Controls the direction of the edges when extruded.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/29954e90-0064-448e-88b2-8470b42ffdbc.mp4" />

### Selection Side [#selection-side]

You can specify which side should be affected (both or front only).

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/47e4693f-64ae-4c2e-a654-600e268af9e7.mp4" />

### Selection Mode [#selection-mode]

You can change the selection mode and adjust the radius of the paint selection

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1f80b23c-6441-4d43-8f46-69e23c675133.mp4" />

### Hole Filling [#hole-filling]

You can specify if the geometry of filled holes will be Flat, Curved or Ngon.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/17c20af8-893b-418e-b110-c7914bcb994d.mp4" />


# 3D Sculpting (/designing-in-3-d/modeling/3d-sculpting)

> Shape organic forms with sculpting brushes, including symmetry and brush settings.



You can sculpt 3D objects with a set of brushes. Easiest way to explore the feature is to create a 3D primitive (e.g.: Sphere, Plane) and click on “Smooth & Edit”. Increase the Subdivisions for a smoother sculpting result. Sculpt is found on the toolbar once an object is in edit mode (access it from right sidebar when an object is selected or simply double-click the object).

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ca29750a-4bb6-48fe-8050-2ba6de293158.mp4" />

The Radius and Strength of the sculpt brush can be adjusted from the right sidebar. Adjust it based on your sculpting needs.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/18abe58f-9f77-403c-9e64-33a93c172b30.png" alt="image" />

***

Following sculpt brush types can be used to manipulate your object:

## Grab [#grab]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5959c0d3-f1a5-4730-9d59-908e3764deb9.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

Elevates the surface from starting point by clicking and dragging. Hold ⌘ or Ctrl to push the surface from starting point.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/66199303-fccd-4866-bded-6603eedcc2ed.mp4" />

## Draw [#draw]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3a9aa0df-aa23-4080-b587-4ced0d7a0e92.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

Click and drag along the surface to elevate it along the path you draw. Hold ⌘ or Ctrl to push the surface.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5c5454b4-c71a-4d44-8606-2e0d158382cf.mp4" />

## Draw Clay [#draw-clay]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/38a72e60-0fc9-42c4-b75b-5c27766a18fe.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

Similar to draw but it has a flat base based on the set radius for the brush. Click and drag to elevate, hold ⌘ or Ctrl to push the surface.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5a93630a-bf43-4f37-81df-65490bc0c0a8.mp4" />

## Smooth [#smooth]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/2e09813c-3bff-4a02-a87b-15ce39374135.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

If your sculpting is rough the Smooth brush makes the surface more even and smooth.
