**Base →** Determines the visibility of the reference object in play mode.

**Position →** Sets the specific position of the created object based on the x, y, and z values.

**Delay** → Specifies the delay in seconds before the object is created.

**Frequency** → Sets the frequency at which the object is created, ranging from 0.01 to 60 times per second.

**Rotation** → Specifies the rotation of the created object based on the x, y, and z values.

**Dynamic** → Determines whether physics is applied to the created object.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b0ac6f7a-3fcc-4c8a-98ee-f783e93ff925.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

**Velocity →** Sets the initial velocity of the created object along the x, y, and z axes.

**Destroy** → Determines when the created object should be destroyed.

* **No:** The object will not be destroyed automatically.
* **After Lifetime:** The object will be destroyed after a specified lifetime in seconds.
* **After Collision:** The object will be destroyed upon collision with another object.
* **After Amount is Reached:** The object will be destroyed after a specific number of instances have been created.
  * **Amount:** Specifies the desired number of instances to be created before the object is destroyed.


# Destroy Object Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/destroy-object-action)



The Destroy Object action lets you interactively destroy/hide objects from your scene.

Destroying objects is possible via various events, like mouse down, collision, or trigger area, which unlocks plenty of use cases for playful effects and interactions.

1. Select an object and create an event.
2. Open the event and find the “Actions” panel
3. Add “Destroy Object” to your actions.
4. Inside the action, you can define which objects will be destroyed when the event is triggered.

After your event is created, open the ‘Edit Event’ panel to customize your Open Link action.

* **URL** - Input the URL where you want to link to.
* **Target** - Decide how the link is going to be opened (Current Tab, New Tab, or New Window)

# Particles Control Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/particles-control-action)

**Emitter** - Select Particle Emitter to control.

**Emission (Start, Stop, Start/Stop, Pause, Pause/Resume, or Reset)** - Decide how to control the emission of the particles.

**Delay** - Time before the event action is triggered.

# Scene Transition Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/scene-transition-action)



A scene transition action allows you to create a transition between multiple scenes on your file.

Learn more about [Multi-Scenes](https://docs.spline.design/designing-in-3-d/scenes/multi-scenes.md).

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5597679b-5676-481d-83a4-879a418e8e90.mp4" />

***

## Creating a new Scene Transition Action [#creating-a-new-scene-transition-action]

To add a new scene transition:

1. Make sure you have multiple scenes in your file.
2. Select the object to which you want to apply the scene transition to.
3. Add an event that will be used to trigger the scene transition action.
4. Open the Event and add a scene transition action.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/11f2e047-43b9-4a3e-be4b-051ca8ced1e4.mp4" />

## Customizing a Scene Transition Action [#customizing-a-scene-transition-action]

Open the ‘Edit Event’ panel to customize your scene transition.

Here are the different customizable sections of a scene transition action:

**Target**

The scene that the event will transition to.

**Transition**

It can be instant or have a fade-in effect.

* None → will result in an instant transition
* Fade-in → will make a fade-in effect with a set duration defined in seconds

**Delay** → A delay can be set before the transition happens.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8d5b76d6-8c72-451a-8041-3d00a61c3b06.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/floatingstonescenesfix-bd6918be45e8faffacb3431eb74ad9b9/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *🙌 **Use*** `WASD&#x60; &#x2A;**to walk, and*** `SPACE&#x60; &#x2A;**to jump***\*. Try out Scene Transition in action with this example by walking into one of the portal platforms.\*


# Set Variable Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/set-variable-action)



Set Variable Action can update the value of a variable defined by an expression. The expression can reference another variable or variable helpers.

<Callout>
  Learn more about [Variables](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables.md) to fully understand how to use Set Variable Action.
</Callout>

## How to use Set Variable Action [#how-to-use-set-variable-action]

When an Event is performed, a variable can be set to a new value in the Set Variable Action by an expression – it can be either updated to another variable or a custom calculation. Following Events have access to Set Variable Action.

Learn how to use Set Variable Action through how-to steps in the [Variables](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables.md) doc.

## Events supporting Set Variable Action [#events-supporting-set-variable-action]

When an Event is performed, a variable can be set to a new value in the Set Variable Action by an expression – it can be either updated to another variable or a custom calculation. Following Events have access to Set Variable Action

[Variable Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/variable-change-event.md)

[Start Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/start-event.md)

[Mouse Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-up-event.md)

[Mouse Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-down-event.md)

[Mouse Hover Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-hover-event.md)

[Key Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-up-event.md)

[Key Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-down-event.md)

[Key Press Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-press-event.md)

[Scroll Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/scroll-event.md)

[Drag and Drop Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/drag-and-drop-event.md)

[Distance Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/distance-event.md)

[State Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/state-change-event.md)

[Collision Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/collision-event.md)

[Trigger Area Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/trigger-area-event.md)

## Set Variable Action properties [#set-variable-action-properties]

### Select Variable [#select-variable]

Select the variable to be updated via the Set Variable Action. The dropdown list will access any variables that are available on the current file.

### Add Expression [#add-expression]

Create an expression to which the selected variable should be updated.

The following variable types can be used:

| **Variable type**              | **Defined by**                                                                                                                                               | **Usage**                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **🤹 Variables**               | Number, String, Boolean, Timers, Counter or Random                                                                                                           | Variables can store values that are reusable. For detailed explanation read the [Variables](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables.md) doc. |
| **ℹ️ Object Property**         | Object’s position, rotation, or scale (X, Y, Z), or it’s width, height and depth as a number, e.g.: `100`                                                    | Capture object property changes, and save them as variables to create advanced interactions.                                                                          |
| **🖱️ Mouse Position (2D)**    | The position of the mouse on the screen is defined by X and Y as numbers.                                                                                    | Animate or manipulate objects based on the mouse position                                                                                                             |
| **💥 Mouse Hit Position (3D)** | The position of the mouse hitting an object in 3D space, as Position X, Y, or Z of the hit location, or the Position X, Y, and Z of the object that was hit. | Create interactions based on specific hit positions, e.g.: visualize hit in 3D space.                                                                                 |

The following math operations can be used in formulas:

| **Symbol** | **Operator**                        |
| ---------- | ----------------------------------- |
| `+`        | Add                                 |
| `-`        | Subtract                            |
| `*`        | Multiply                            |
| `/`        | Divide                              |
| `Random`   | Random value                        |
| `(` `)`    | Group calculations with parenthesis |

Some example formulas to spark ideas

* Variable A + Variable B
* Variable A \* 10 / 2
* (Variable A + Variable B) \* 10

***

## Using Set Variable Action with Conditional Action [#using-set-variable-action-with-conditional-action]

[Conditional Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/conditional-action.md) allows you to create more sophisticated Set Variable Actions. Read the doc to learn more about it.


# Sound Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/sound-action)



A sound action will play a sound when triggered by an event. Adding sounds to your scenes is a great way of making them more immersive and rich.

<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/h1lxi_sFIgY" title="YouTube video player" />

***

## Creating a new Sound Action [#creating-a-new-sound-action]

Here's how to add sounds to your 3D objects:

1. Select the object that you want to add a sound to. (Tip: If you want to add background music or environment sound you can create an empty group object)
2. On the Events panel, add the event that will trigger the sound action.
3. Open the Event and add a sound action.

<Callout>
  Spline’s Sound Library access is available on **Hobby**, **Pro** and **Max** plans.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3d864569-04c7-4ba5-aec6-6cf821dcd5dc.mp4" />

## Customizing a Sound Action [#customizing-a-sound-action]

#### Play a Sound [#play-a-sound]

After your event is created, open the ‘Edit Event’ panel to customize your sound action.

* **Interaction** - Decide if you want to Play, Stop or Pause a sound.
* **After** - Decide when the sound is played.
* **Sound File** - .mp3 or .wav formats, max 2MB.
* **Volume** - Controls the volume of the sound.
* **Delay** - Controls the delay of when the sound is played.
* **Loop** - Define if you want the sound to play once, infinitely, or set the number of times to be played.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/adc5ea4d-aab5-43b1-98c7-8430690557aa.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

#### Stop / Pause a Sound [#stop--pause-a-sound]

When stopping or pausing a sound, the properties are a bit different.

* **Interaction** - Decide if you want to Play, Stop or Pause a sound.
* **Location** - The object where the sound you want to stop/pause is located at.
* **Sound File** - The sound you want to stop/pause.
* **Delay** - Controls the delay of when the sound is stopped/paused.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1bdf16bd-7a40-4aed-aa11-62a349215179.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />


# Switch Camera Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/switch-camera-action)



The Switch Camera action allows you to switch between different cameras via the event system.

## Creating a new Switch Camera Action [#creating-a-new-switch-camera-action]

1\. Select an object and create an event on the events panel. 2. Open the event and find the “Actions” panel. 3. Add “Switch Camera” to your actions. 4. Select which camera to switch to and customize the settings to your liking.

## Customizing a Switch Camera Action [#customizing-a-switch-camera-action]

* **Target →** Select the camera you want to switch to.
* **Mode →** If it switches once or if you can toggle between the target camera and the original camera.
* **Delay** → Add a delay before the camera switch.
* **Animate** → Decide if the transition between cameras is animated or instant. If Animate = Yes, you can then customize the transition curve and duration.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/58446e28-1927-44b3-8abc-b4e23e3b7222.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/8F77LHO5Lvb-bP16rKfkxNNT/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Press the monitor to switch between Camera 1 and Camera 2. Notice how it animates smoothly and how you can orbit even on Camera 2.*


# Transition Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/transition-action)



Transitions are used to make an object transition between multiple states and they are a core function of adding interactivity and animation to your scenes. A transition is an action of an event.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8d5547c7-0ada-436a-b971-1be4bb304c26.mp4" />

### Creating a new Transition Action [#creating-a-new-transition-action]

To add a new transition you first need to create multiple states of your object and add an event that will trigger the transition action.

1. Select the object to which you want to apply a transition to.
2. Create at least one new state where you’ll transition to.
3. Add an event to the object.
4. Open the Event and add a transition action.

<Callout type="idea">
  Create transitions between multiple states for more complex sequential animations.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8343c323-4f78-48af-a8a9-74d482c32ecc.mp4" />

***

### Customizing a Transition Action [#customizing-a-transition-action]

After your states and events are created, open the ‘Edit Event’ panel to customize your transitions.

Here are the different customizable sections of a transition action:

#### Target [#target]

The target is the object where you want the transition to happen.

#### Transitions [#transitions]

In the transitions section, you can define what states the transition will go through.

As well as defining the duration and transition properties for each individual transition.

#### Sequence [#sequence]

The sequence properties will affect the whole sequence defined in the Transitions section.

* **Delay** - how many seconds the sequence should wait before it’s played.
* **Loop Settings** - Count - for specific loop amount, Infinite - for looping forever.
* **Cycle** - If the sequence should return to its original starting point.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d55f3ddc-cb97-478d-b666-d4c2c6c52b72.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

#### Transition Properties [#transition-properties]

When pressing the transition curve between states, you can open the properties of each individual transition.

* **Transition Curve** - Linear, Ease, Ease In, Ease Out, Ease In Out, Bezier, Spring.
* **Duration** - Transition duration in seconds from ‘State A’ to ‘State B’.
* **Delay** - how many seconds the animation should wait before it’s played.
* **Loop Settings** - Count - for specific loop amount, Infinite - for looping forever.
* **Cycle** - If the transition should return to its original starting point.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b6ae72ae-9069-4dc8-8dcf-08a987b25bda.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/spheressequentialcopy-1633f2db27cb608f0f8717391a2620d1/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Sequential Transitions in action! ☝️ Drag to Orbit*


# Variable Control Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/variable-control-action)



Variable Control Action allows you to control the interaction of dynamic variables in the scene.

<Callout>
  Learn more about [Variables](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables.md) to fully understand how to use Variable Control Action.
</Callout>

## How to use Variable Control Action [#how-to-use-variable-control-action]

When an Event is performed, a dynamic variable can be controlled with the following interaction types: `Play`, `Pause`, `Stop` , `Restart`, or `Toggle - Ping Pong` along with a delay value.

Here is a simple how-to example of interacting with the dynamic variable via a mouse click.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/86e552f7-30ff-404f-a428-2ba994a97182.mp4" />

*Video demonstrating how to use Variable Control Action with Dynamic Counter Variable.*

1. In a new scene, create a cube and a dynamic variable of type `Counter` from the Variable panel.
2. Set the Interval value to “0.001” to make the counter grow faster.
3. Select the cube and assign the counter variable to the X position of the cube.
4. Add an event in the right sidebar in the events section by clicking the “+” icon.

   <Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3113706f-18b5-463b-91e0-57da66c6a0d5.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />
5. Change the event type in the top dropdown to “Mouse Down”.
6. Add an action “Variable Control” and expand it by clicking on the “>” icon.
7. Click on the dropdown in the Variable Control Action and select the variable to be controlled –  the `Counter` for this example.
8. Enter Play mode and click on the cube to see it moving along the X axis over time.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/43958c6f-b33a-420b-a391-ff0c62c42e58.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

## Events supporting Variable Control Action [#events-supporting-variable-control-action]

The following Events have access to Variable Control Action.

* [Start Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/start-event.md)
* [Mouse Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-up-event.md)
* [Mouse Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-down-event.md)
* [Mouse Press Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-press-event.md)
* [Mouse Hover Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-hover-event.md)
* [Key Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-up-event.md)
* [Key Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-down-event.md)
* [Key Press Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-press-event.md)
* [Scroll Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/scroll-event.md)
* [Drag and Drop Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/drag-and-drop-event.md)
* [Variable Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/variable-change-event.md)
* [Distance Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/distance-event.md)
* [State Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/state-change-event.md)
* [Collision Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/collision-event.md)
* [Trigger Area Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/trigger-area-event.md)
* [Screen Resize Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/screen-resize-event.md)

***

## Variable Control Action properties [#variable-control-action-properties]

### Select Dynamic Variable [#select-dynamic-variable]

Select the dynamic variable to be updated via the Variable Control Action. The dropdown list will access any dynamic variables that are available in the current file.

### Interaction [#interaction]

Set the type of interaction that should occur when an event is performed.

**Play** → Plays the dynamic variable.

**Pause** → Pauses the dynamic variable.

**Play/Pause** → On event trigger, plays or pauses the dynamic variable based on the current state.

**Stop** → Stops the running dynamic variable.

**Restart** → Restarts the dynamic variable.

**Toggle - Ping Pong** → Reverses the dynamic variable.

### Delay [#delay]

Adds a delay to the interaction of the Variable Control Action to take place.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/20dd50c4-d8e4-4ad9-93d9-1f1bbb7008c0.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Callout>
  Restart and Toggle interactions are only available for Counter Dynamic Variable.
</Callout>

***

## Using Variable Control with Conditional Action [#using-variable-control-with-conditional-action]

[Conditional Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/conditional-action.md) allows you to create more sophisticated Variable Control Actions. Read the doc to learn more about it.


# Video Action (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/video-action)



A video action will play a video layer when triggered by an event. Adding video to your scenes is a great way of making them more immersive and rich.

A video action needs a Video Layer to play, pause or stop. A video layer is a material layer that lets you add a video as a texture to your 2D and 3D objects.

<Callout>
  Uploading videos is a paid feature, you’ll need to have an active **Starter, Professional** or **Team** subscription to use it.
</Callout>

Learn more about the [Video Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/video-layer.md).

## Creating a new Video Action [#creating-a-new-video-action]

1. Select the object that has the video layer you want to play, stop or pause.
2. On the Events panel, add the event that will trigger the video action.
3. Open the Event and add a video action.

<Callout type="idea">
  When adding a new Video Layer, a Start Event is automatically created with a Video Action, making your video autoplay on scene load.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f2fa00b3-8eb7-415e-a753-f8f7c2350cb5.mp4" />

## Customizing a Video Action [#customizing-a-video-action]

After your event is created, open the ‘Edit Event’ panel to customize your video action.

* **Interaction** - Decide if you want to Play, Stop or Pause a video.
* **After** - Decide when the video is played.
* **Video File** - The video layer you want to target.
* **Volume** - Controls the volume of the video.
* **Delay** - Controls the delay of when the video is played.
* **Loop** - Define if you want the video to play once, infinitely, or set the number of times to be played.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f4eb4c89-f173-4d53-b3cb-e335a3f9ba7e.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/iphone14provideo-f43255dbccafd395e683fd56d508fb0c/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Video Layer and Video Action! ☝️ Drag to Orbit & Press to Interact*


# Animatable Properties (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/animatable-properties)



Here's a list of properties that can be animated using states and events in Spline.

### All Objects [#all-objects]

* Position, Rotation, Scale
* Size (X, Y, Z)
* Extrusion
* Material Layers

### Particle Emitter & Force [#particle-emitter--force]

* Position, Rotation

### Cameras [#cameras]

* Position, Rotation, Scale
* Zoom

### Light Objects [#light-objects]

* Position, Rotation, Scale
* Color, Intensity

### 3D Paths [#3d-paths]

* Position, Rotation, Scale
* Depth, Offset
* Angel, Twist
* Start Scale, End Scale

### Align to Path [#align-to-path]

* Offset
* Slide

### Cloner Properties [#cloner-properties]

| Radial Type | Linear Type | Grid Type |   | Randomness |
| ----------- | ----------- | --------- | - | ---------- |
| Count       | Count       | Count     |   | Strength   |
| Position    | Position    | Size      |   | Position   |
| Scale       | Scale       |           |   | Scale      |
| Rotation    | Rotation    |           |   | Rotation   |
| Radius      |             |           |   | Seed       |
| Start       |             |           |   | Movement   |
| End         |             |           |   | Scale      |

# Events & Interactivity (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/events-interactivity)



<Callout>
  Events enable you to change an [object state](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/how-state-based-animation-works.md) based on your input to the Viewport; this is similar to how CSS or JS events work (if you are familiar with these terms).
</Callout>

Events are how you can trigger actions like transitions to have animation and interactivity.

#### Spline Events [#spline-events]

To learn more about each specific event, see its page:

* [Start Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/start-event.md)
* [Mouse Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-up-event.md)
* [Mouse Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-down-event.md)
* [Mouse Press Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-press-event.md)
* [Mouse Hover Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-hover-event.md)
* [Key Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-up-event.md)
* [Key Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-down-event.md)
* [Key Press Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-press-event.md)
* [Scroll Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/scroll-event.md)
* [Look At Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/look-at-event.md)
* [Follow Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/follow-event.md)
* [Game Controls Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/game-controls-event.md)
* [Distance Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/distance-event.md)
* [State Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/state-change-event.md)
* [Variable Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/variable-change-event.md)
* [Collision Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/collision-event.md)
* [Drag and Drop Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/drag-and-drop-event.md)
* [Trigger Area Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/trigger-area-event.md)
* [Screen Resize Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/screen-resize-event.md)
* [API Updated Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/api-updated-event.md)
* [Webhook Called Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/webhook-called-event.md)
* [AI Assistant Listener Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-listener-event.md)
* [AI Assistant Trigger Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-trigger-event.md)

***

## Creating your first Event & Action [#creating-your-first-event--action]

1. Select the object you want to add a new event to.
2. Go to the Events panel on the right sidebar and click the `+` icon.
3. Open the Event created and if necessary, use the top dropdown to switch to a different event type.
4. Create a new action by choosing one of the options available in the empty state.
5. From the Target dropdown, choose the object you want to target for the event.
6. In the Transitions section, you can build your sequence of transitions between different states.

You can now play around with the rest of the settings and preview your animation by entering Play Mode.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/e8389604-8924-456b-bef3-161ec8cb3a63.mp4" />

*Example: Creating a "Start" Event cycling and looping with a "Spring" transition curve.*

> *Click & Drag 👆 – A "Start" Event cycling and looping with a "Spring" transition curve.*


# How state-based animation works (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/how-state-based-animation-works)



When you want to start adding animation and interactions to your scene, it's time to understand &#x2A;**States,**&#x2A; &#x2A;**Events, and Actions***.

State-based animation works by adding, well, you guessed it, multiple states to your objects.

### How to create an object state [#how-to-create-an-object-state]

1. Select an object.
2. Go to the **States** panel on the right sidebar and click the `+` icon.
3. You'll see two new states. The `Base State` (the state your object was already in) and another one called `State`.
4. The `State` will be automatically selected, meaning you're ready to start applying changes to your object, like changing the scale, position, rotation, color, etc.
5. Once you made changes to `State`, you can switch between `Base State` and `State` on the States panel to see the differences.

*Read also:*\
[Animatable Properties](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/animatable-properties.md)

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/baf34298-4889-47d0-ac88-311dd22dbe18.mp4" />

You have a new state, but when you enter &#x2A;*Play Mode ("Play" icon)**, nothing happens. That's when Events and Actions come into play. Continue to the next doc to learn more.


# Input (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/input)



An Input object lets you create a dynamic input field. It’s a great way to connect with API, Webhooks or OpenAI API to send user-created text or prompts.

[OpenAI API](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/ai-text-open-ai-api.md)

[Real-time API](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/real-time-api.md)

[Webhooks](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/webhooks.md)

To create an Input follow these steps:

1. Click on the `+` icon in the editor’s toolbar
2. Select the `Input object` in the list.
3. Drag an area in the viewport to define the input field’s area
4. Define the text properties, to learn more about working with text read the following doc: [Working with Text](https://docs.spline.design/designing-in-3-d/objects/working-with-text.md)


# Mouse & Key Toggle Property (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/mouse-key-toggle-property)

> Reverse an animation on the second click or key press, without building a second state.



This property allows you to easily reverse the animation of an event so each time you use a mouse or key event it’s going to switch from one state to another. This is useful to create interactions like toggle buttons.

### How to use the Toggle property in Mouse and Key events: [#how-to-use-the-toggle-property-in-mouse-and-key-events]

1. Select the object and add new states to it using the States panel. You can change the position or how it looks accordingly.
2. On the Events panels, add one of the following events that support toggle mode for the transition action: Mouse Down, Mouse Up, Key Down, Key Up, Collision, or Trigger Area.
3. Add a transition action to the Mouse Down event, and change the target object if needed.
4. Change the mode to “Toggle” - This will enable you to do the reverse when the object is clicked.
5. Add the transition steps to the action that you want to toggle between.
6. That’s it - Enter Play Mode to test.

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/switchtutorial-70a23dd070513899912c011c202b0368/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

<Callout>
  *Test it! Press the switch above 👆*
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/85f41b24-1c21-4db5-aa39-6d30660429a0.mp4" />

> *Click the link to learn how to create this 3D Switch design: [Watch YouTube Tutorial](https://youtu.be/lJIDSiDlhvw?si=_3R5qCeGji_XrCoi)*


# AI Assistant Listener Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-listener-event)



The AI Assistant Listener event is used in conjunction with the [AI Voice Assistant API](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/ai-voice-assistant-api.md) and it's an event that is constantly listening for changes in the audio spectrum of the AI Assistant.

***

## AI Assistant Listener Event Properties [#ai-assistant-listener-event-properties]

Since this event is designed to work with the AI Assistant’s audio spectrum, it is only compatible with **Set Variable** actions. You can use a **Set Variable** action to apply the audio spectrum to one of your variables or modify any other variables that depend on the audio spectrum.

You can then use those variables to manipulate properties of your objects like position, rotation, scale, etc.

### Frequency [#frequency]

In frequency, you can select different ways of using the voice audio waves.

**Average** - Uses an average value from all the frequencies.

**Range** - Uses the average of a range of frequencies.

**Single** - Uses a single frequency.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/7265a2dc-0bcd-4859-b277-6d9823c362de.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

Learn more about related topics:

* [AI Voice Assistant API](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/ai-voice-assistant-api.md)
* [Variables](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables.md)


# AI Assistant Trigger Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-trigger-event)



The AI Assistant Trigger event is used in conjunction with the [AI Voice Assistant API](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/ai-voice-assistant-api.md) and allows you to trigger a set of actions when the user prompts the AI with something similar to the description of the trigger event.

***

## AI Assistant Trigger Event Properties [#ai-assistant-trigger-event-properties]

#### Trigger Description [#trigger-description]

In order for the AI to recognize which event to trigger, it will need a description of what the actions inside that event do.

For example, if we want the AI to move the blue cube in our scene, we can describe the trigger event as "move blue cube". When the AI is prompted to move the blue cube with natural language, it will understand that it needs to trigger the event with the description that matches the ask from the user.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/a30ed879-65b5-4686-99c9-47bcd8432b6f.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

Refer below for all the actions you can trigger with the AI Assistant Trigger Event:

* [Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/transition-action.md)
* [Animation Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/animation-action.md)
* [Sound Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/sound-action.md)
* [Particle Control Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/particles-control-action.md)
* [Video Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/video-action.md)

* [Create Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/create-object-action.md)
* [Destroy Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/destroy-object-action.md)
* [Switch Camera Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/switch-camera-action.md)
* [Scene Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/scene-transition-action.md)
* [Reset Scene Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/reset-scene-action.md)

Once the API Request is finished, the API Updated event will trigger the Actions added to it.

The dropdown list will access any APIs available on the current file.

# Collision Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/collision-event)



With Collision Events and Physics, it is possible to trigger events when objects hit other objects. In combination with Game Controls, you can create more interactive experiences.

### How to add a Collision Event [#how-to-add-a-collision-event]

1. For Collision Events to work, it is needed to have Physics Enabled on the environment as well as on the objects. Go to Environment settings and enable Physics, then select the object(s) to enable physics on them as well.
2. Select the object(s) and create a new event under the Events Panel.
3. Open the Collision event to start adding and tweaking actions and parameters. The following parameters can be adjusted:
   * **With -** Select with what the collision event will be triggered: *Characters* (objects with Game Control events), and *Anything* (any object of the scene).
   * **Toggle -** Allows reversing the animation of an event so each time a collision happens, the event will trigger an action and reverse it.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9d844e49-09c3-48a3-8dd8-1f9d52ec3134.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Callout>
  To learn more about the Toggle option, go to [Mouse & Key Toggle Property](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/mouse-key-toggle-property.md).
</Callout>

#### Actions [#actions]

Similar to other events, Collision events can trigger transitions that target objects and trigger a state change with transitions, delay, cycle, and loop properties. Sound actions can play, pause, and stop sounds from Spline’s Sounds Library or your own custom sounds.

<Callout>
  *A Starter, Professional or Team subscription is needed to access Spline’s Sounds Library.*
</Callout>

Here’s how to add Collision Events to your scene.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/e15b5ef1-84ed-4c7c-b357-5b6fdb8170fd.mp4" />

*Adding Collision Events.*


# Distance Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/distance-event)



The Distance Event allows you to trigger actions based on the distance between two objects.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/11cd135b-84d2-497f-b0f5-279086308b0d.mp4" />

Here are the different properties of a Distance Event.

* **From** - Define object 1 for the distance check.
* **To** - Define object 2 for the distance check.
* **Distance** - The set value of distance between object 1 and 2 where the actions will be triggered.

If the distance between objects is less than the set value, the actions under ‘Then’ will be triggered.

And if it becomes more than the set value, the actions under ‘Else’ will be triggered.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/725f5a37-54e1-4efc-a5ce-d07eb3cfaf6c.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/AUrtEhEYVTqPM5J5RpYGvihK/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *👆 Try it! Press the canvas to focus and use WASD to control the Sphere.*


# Drag and Drop Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/drag-and-drop-event)



Drag and Drop Event allows you to create interactive experiences where objects can be dragged and dropped with customizable parameters.

<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/YZyxoDXoOyc" title="YouTube video player" />

***

## Adding a Drag and Drop Event [#adding-a-drag-and-drop-event]

<Callout>
  By customizing parameters and behaviors, you can create dynamic drag and drop interactions in your scenes.
</Callout>

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f06b6a8e-76ff-47d5-a552-b254eb6b070d.png" alt="image" />

Here's how to use the Drag and Drop Event:

1. Select the object(s) that you want to make draggable.
2. On the Events panel, add a new event and select `Drag and Drop` from the dropdown menu.
3. Open the `Drag and Drop` event and customize the settings to your liking.

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/UQe8GKoXSTQP4rG1rCZ9i5Rx/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

<Callout>
  *Use your mouse/touchscreen to interact! 👆 You can drag and drop the purple cubes. Tip: You can snap them on the grey shapes.*
</Callout>

### Settings for Drag [#settings-for-drag]

**Cursor**

* Hand / Move / Default → Affects cursor appearance while hovering over draggable objects.

**Render**

* Normal: the draggable object will be covered with other objects.
* On Top of All: Renders the dragged objects on top of all other objects.

**Damping**

* Sets the damping value for the dragged objects. Values range from 1 to 80, where higher values provide more delay.

**Plane**

* Aligned with Camera: The drag plane is aligned with the camera's orientation.
* Adaptive to Camera: The drag plane adapts to the camera's orientation as it moves.
* Axis or Plane: The drag plane is aligned with the selected axis or plane.

**Plane Mode**

* Keep Initial Plane Position: The drag plane retains its initial position.
* Use Current Object Position: The drag plane uses the current position of the dragged object.

**Relative To**

* World: The drag is relative to the world coordinates.
* Parent: The drag is relative to the parent object's coordinates.
* Object: The drag is relative to the object's own coordinates.

**Objects to Drag**

* Add objects to the list that you want to make draggable.

**Drag Limits**

* Define the minimum and maximum values for the X, Y, and Z axes to limit the dragging range. Values can be arbitrary, ranging from negative to positive.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/546fb4e8-231c-409d-a47e-e49b28ecf538.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

### Settings for Drop [#settings-for-drop]

Drop allows objects to snap to specific positions after being dropped. Drop is an optional feature. Turn it on to take effect. Customize the following properties:

**Snap to**

* Object Position: Snaps the dropped object to the position of another object.
* Surface: Snaps the dropped object to the surface it is dropped on.

**Snap Speed**

* Sets the speed at which the object snaps to the target position. Values range from 0 to 40, with higher values indicating faster snapping.

**Auto-Orient**

* Yes: The dropped object automatically orients itself to match the target object or surface.
* No: The dropped object maintains its original orientation.

**Fallback**

* Stay in Dropped Position: The dropped object remains in the position it was dropped.
* Return to Original Position: The dropped object returns to its original position.
* Return to Last Snapped Position: The dropped object returns to the last snapped position.

**Drop On**

* Any Object: The dropped object can be dropped on any object in the scene.
* List of Objects: Define a list of specific objects that the dropped object can be dropped on.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/6df344fe-d7bd-49f6-94dc-d3578aab5fe9.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

### Behavior on Drag and Drop [#behavior-on-drag-and-drop]

Add actions such as transitions, sounds, or creating objects that will occur when the drag and drop event is triggered.

The following actions can be added for Drag and Drop events:

* [Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/transition-action.md) → e.g.: change the state of an object if it is Dragged or Dropped.
* [Sound Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/sound-action.md) → e.g.: add a sound effect when dragging or dropping the object.
* [Create Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/create-object-action.md) → e.g.: spawn additional objects in the scene when the object is Dragged or Dropped.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/4e88ea41-35f8-4e57-9996-603880fdb535.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />


# Follow Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/follow-event)



The Follow Event is used to create interactions where objects will follow either the cursor or another object.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b2b3ba4e-61a4-48d2-8f6d-9048c7059536.mp4" />

Here are the different properties of a Follow Event.

* **Target** - What the object will follow, either the cursor or another object.
* **Plane** - To what plane should the object movement be restricted to.
* **Damping** - Makes the follow effect lag behind whatever it is tracking.
* **Offset** - a distance offset from the cursor to the object that is following it.
* **Translation** - on what axis will the following object move.
* **View Reset** - If the object should reset back to its original position or not when the mouse is out of the canvas.
* **Speed** - The speed of the reset to the original position, if hover out is selected.
* **Create Object Action** - while the following object is moving you can trigger a create object action.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/09e5f796-6e01-4865-bbe8-30d9b0c3932c.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

If following another object there are also the Distance Limit properties.

* **Distance** - The distance of where the follow event will work in, past that distance the object will stop following.
* **Delay** - the amount of time before the object starts following the other if within the defined distance.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d85bd057-c7e2-4f0d-bb05-78d48454b76a.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/cBQiVCSdfAPEs7-eCW0TsJj/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Use your mouse to interact! 👆 The Follow Event can make objects follow either the cursor or another object.*


# Game Controls Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/game-controls-event)



With a Game Controls event, you can create interactive experiences, where you control a character (that’s what we call the controlled object), by walking or flying around your scenes.

Navigate your scene and control your character with key inputs, joysticks and buttons on mobile, gravity, and collision detection.

There are many different options to customize it to the type of experience you decide to build.

***

## Test the gameplay 🎮👇 [#test-the-gameplay-]

Before moving on with the documentation, why not give the game controls a quick test below?

Use `W S A D` keys to move the character, `SPACE` to jump, and `↑↓←→` keys to move the camera.

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/pigislandtutorialcopy-421e2ff873658c086c6846c5d9b79317/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

<Callout>
  *Click the embed to focus. Open the experience in a new tab [HERE](https://my.spline.design/pigislandtutorialcopy-421e2ff873658c086c6846c5d9b79317/).*
</Callout>

***

## Game Controls Video Tutorial [#game-controls-video-tutorial]

If you prefer to follow along with a video, we’ve made this tutorial where we go through all the details of a Game Controls event.

<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/yvehPjQhS5U" title="YouTube video player" />

***

## Adding a Game Controls Event [#adding-a-game-controls-event]

Here's how to add a Game Controls event to an object in your scene:

1. Select the object that you want to control.
2. On the Events panel, add a new event and select `Game Controls` from the dropdown menu.
3. Open the Game Controls event and tweak the settings to your liking.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/a7427866-2f8b-4151-8400-0609a95decd2.mp4" />

## Adjusting Character Orientation [#adjusting-character-orientation]

If your character is composed of grouped objects and you notice it is moving backwards, you’ll need to make sure it is facing towards the -Z direction.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/dbb612f0-3d77-4417-87c0-7286c25efd6f.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

To fix this you’ll need to select all the objects inside your main group and rotate them to face the -Z direction.

This usually means rotating all the objects inside your character group by 180º degrees.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8ebb478f-c5cd-4b02-b557-99aec482b096.mp4" />

*Adjusting the orientation of a character so it faces the -Z direction.*

***

## Understanding the Game Controls Settings [#understanding-the-game-controls-settings]

Let's now dive deep into all the settings that can be found inside the Game Controls event, so you can adjust them to properly match the experience you’re building.

### Movement [#movement]

In Movement, you can define how your character will be moving around your scene.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/43ca1af9-3c8a-4a85-8280-b254ae4bcc39.mp4" />

*Walk or Fly are the two movement modes of a Game Controls event.*

**Movement Mode**

* **Walk** - is used for experiences where you move on surfaces.
* **Fly** - is used for experiences where you fly around your scene.

**Auto-Orientation**

When turned on, the character rotates automatically based on the direction you’re moving in.

**Speed**

Control the speed of position and rotation.

**Delay**

Control the acceleration at the Start and deceleration at the End of position and rotation.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ea8ccef7-a1d3-4e7a-8dbe-94fdde605897.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

## Controls [#controls]

To create unique game mechanics controls can be adjusted.

Desktop Controls is where we can define what keys are going to be used to control our character and what each of them does.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/e73684fc-386e-4def-bec1-7ab4deae8e5b.mp4" />

*Remapping a key and changing its action.*

**Rotate By**

Define how you can rotate the camera. Either by using the Mouse, the Arrow keys, or both.

**Keyboard Keys**

Once you select a key, you can either press another key to assign it or change the action of that key on the dropdown below.

If you’ve made too many changes, the reset button (on the top right) will let you reset to the default keys.

Here’s a list of all the behaviors and their default keys:

| Default Key | Behavior          |
| ----------- | ----------------- |
| W           | Move Forward (-Z) |
| S           | Move Back (+Z)    |
| A           | Move Left (-X)    |
| D           | Move Right (+X)   |
| ↑           | Rotate Up (+X)    |
| ↓           | Rotate Down (-X)  |
| ←           | Rotate Left (+Y)  |
| →           | Rotate Right (-Y) |
|             |                   |
| Space       | Jump              |
| Ctrl        | None by default   |
| Shift       | Run               |
| None        | Move Up (+Y)      |
| None        | Move Down (-Y)    |

### Multiple Game Controls Events [#multiple-game-controls-events]

If you have multiple Game Controls events in your scene, only one of them will be the one where you can define the desktop and mobile controls.

So on every new Game Controls event, you create, you’ll see this warning, letting you know that the event you’re editing is not the main Game Controls event.

But you can always press the button below to make it the main one and edit the controls from there.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/7608ce10-a07c-4e24-801b-76f6b4916b5e.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

## Touch Controls [#touch-controls]

Touch Controls lets you define the controls of your experience on touch-based devices like phones and tablets.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/cde48552-c64a-4ffd-bdf6-46424fb1c097.mp4" />

*How touch controls look on a touch-based device.*

**Rotate By**

Define how you can rotate the camera. Either by dragging on the screen or by using a joystick.

**Joysticks and Buttons Position**

Choose where to position the joysticks and buttons for **Move**, **Rotate** (only visible if Rotate By is set to Joystick), and **Jump**.

The representation you see is how they will be positioned on the device’s screen.

**Offset** - The space the control has from the screen edge.

**Button Size** - The size of each control.

**Visibility** - Defines if the selected control is always visible or becomes visible when interacted with.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/118f7168-3365-4fc4-b3ba-a33817a8c6ed.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

## Collision [#collision]

With collision, we can define how our character will collide with everything in our scene.

Make changes to the collider on your character and tweak settings like Gravity, Jump Power, and the Reset Position.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9b99ac20-46ee-4c0c-9410-0fad1af8bf7f.mp4" />

*Resizing a capsule collider and testing it in Play Mode.*

**Collider**

Define the boundaries of your character by choosing a collider like a Capsule or a Sphere, and match it perfectly by changing its size, position and rotation.

**Jump**

Define how high your character will jump.

**Reset Y Position**

Define the value on the Y axis where if the character reaches it, the scene will reset.

This is useful for scenes with platforms which the character can fall from.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/53aeb91a-30ef-458c-8d41-c641f788b653.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

**To control the Gravity of your scene**

Unselect all objects and find the scene settings on the right sidebar.

On Simulation, you’ll find the Gravity property.

This affects how your character jumps and falls. As you make the value closer to 0, the character will float more.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/cf650be8-0d97-4afb-9ee9-e97857404dd0.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

## Click To Move - Navigation Mesh [#click-to-move---navigation-mesh]

A navigation mesh is like a map that helps characters or objects move around in a 3D environment. It's made up of connected polygons that represent walkable areas. These polygons are like puzzle pieces that fit together, creating a navigation mesh to show where characters can go without getting stuck.

When instructed to move, Characters use the navigation mesh to find the best path, avoid obstacles and move smoothly in the 3D environment.

With this feature turned on, you can now press around your scene to tell your character where to move to.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/93eb2298-16b4-4c4c-ad27-87f3eadf8519.mp4" />

Click To Move is a component of the Game Controls event and you can toggle it ON from the event settings.

**Grid Size** → How big the polygons that make up the navigation mesh are. This allows you to fine-tune your navigation mesh.

**Offset** → Adds spacing along edges or walls so the character doesn’t walk up to them.

**Helper** → Visibility of the wireframe of your navigation mesh.

**Radius** → Radius of the click circle.

**Color** → Color of the click circle.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1a1c6240-32f5-4f6f-85ab-12b51b207728.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

#### Zones [#zones]

**All** → Will use all objects from the scene to generate the navigation mesh.

**Custom** → Gives you control over what objects to use when generating the navigation mesh.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/278e5cd2-9aa2-416e-a86a-332c44f0ad65.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

## Camera Follow [#camera-follow]

If you need a camera to be following your character, this is where you can define which one will be used for it.

<Callout>
  So you stay organized, better collaborate with others and avoid accidental changes, we recommend that you create a new camera for this purpose and use your Personal Camera to move around in the editor.
</Callout>

To create a new camera, you can do so from the “+” icon on the toolbar and selecting Camera, or from the Camera Panel and then pressing “Add new camera” from the dropdown.

Make sure to position the camera in the position you want the experience to be viewed by everyone in the final result.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b8454db6-ac2b-4700-9b42-5cd34a676c8d.mp4" />

*Difference between a static Camera and a Camera following the character.*

**Camera**

Select the camera that is going to be following your character.

**Orient To**

**Normal** - When moving the character the camera will follow it by moving forward, backwards, or pan to the sides.

**Angle** - When moving the character the camera will follow it by moving but also rotate at an angle behind the character.

**Up/Down & Left/Right**

With the directions Up, Down, Left, and Right, you can decide how the user can control the camera.

You can either allow the user to move it in those directions, disable it, or define limits by using angle values.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/a7ae6d1a-9b9f-45d6-aaf8-d78aa3e6f4b6.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

**Matching Cameras**

Remember that the camera used to follow the character should also be the camera defined as the Play Camera in the export settings.

If they’re not, you’ll see this warning, where pressing the button will match the two.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/0db5ff98-db6e-4ed3-b712-a2d470f3b37c.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

If the cameras are all correctly set up, you’ll see this in the export panel, letting you know that the camera is being controlled by a Game Controls event.

Meaning that the camera defined as the Play Camera is currently used to follow a character.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/965a74f2-2f6d-41b0-b40c-387389e00a5a.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

## Behaviors [#behaviors]

With Behaviors, it is possible to add actions to the character when on Idle, when moving, and when jumping, that way creating more interactive reactions to it.

#### Adding Behaviors [#adding-behaviors]

To add behaviors to your character do the following:

1. Open the Game Controls event of your character(s), scroll down to the Behavior section, and open the behavior to which you want to add an action to. The current behaviors are the following:

   * **Idle -** Actions for when the character is in a resting state.
   * **On Move -** Actions for when the character is moving.
   * **On Jump -** Actions for when the character is jumping.
   * **On Run** - Actions for when the character is running

   <Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/7e5800f7-6638-4218-b2fb-cc4d2bf76af9.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />
2. Following actions are supported:

* [Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/transition-action.md)
* [Animation Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/animation-action.md)
* [Sound Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/sound-action.md)
* [Video Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/video-action.md)
* [Create Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/create-object-action.md)

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ef6d973a-be6e-4cc2-9daa-1645cf9a5548.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

3. Create an action on the behavior that you want to adjust and select the object and the state target. Similar to other events, Behaviors can trigger transitions that target objects and trigger a state change with transitions, delay, cycle, and loop properties. Sound actions can play, pause, and stop sounds from Spline’s Original Sounds Library or your own custom sounds.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/c3c1261e-2c44-4437-b714-d49aa9c37eba.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Callout>
  We recommend adding the behavior states in new groups of your character to have better control over them.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/e2dbc29e-6827-4ead-b324-a9a2e2e7975f.mp4" />

*Adding Behaviors to the character.*

# Look At Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/look-at-event)



The Look At Event is used to create interactions where objects will face toward either the cursor or another object.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d8c5093e-2549-49b2-a83f-fd3c1a9e8a3f.mp4" />

Here are the different properties of a Look At Event:

* **Target** - Where is the object looking at.
* **Plane** - To what plane should the object movement be restricted to.
* **Distance** - Intensity of the object rotation based on the distance to target.
* **Direction** - What axis should the object look towards.
* **Damping** - Makes the look at effect lag behind whatever it is tracking.
* **Reset If** - If the object should reset back to its original position or not when the mouse is out of the canvas.

***

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/OcELayVBav1-Zk2TZlgWUZoF/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Use your mouse to interact! 👆 The Look At Event can make objects face toward either the cursor or another object.*

# Screen Resize Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/screen-resize-event)



A Screen Resize Event will trigger actions based on the size of the canvas. Define multiple sizes by adding breakpoints. This is useful when needing to rearrange your scene to better fit different types of screen sizes like desktop vs. mobile.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8545c0f1-22eb-4b1d-8925-a854256a0043.mp4" />

***

### Settings for Screen Resize Event [#settings-for-screen-resize-event]

#### Type [#type]

On type, you can define if the breakpoints on the event will be horizontal or vertical.

#### Breakpoints [#breakpoints]

Breakpoints are defined by pixel values and an operator (`> more than`, `< less than`, and `<> between`).

<Callout>
  Eg. On the UI to the right, on the Mobile preset, the actions will be triggered once the canvas becomes less than 480 pixels horizontally.
</Callout>

* There are 3 presets available (Mobile, Table, Desktop) or you can set your Custom values.
* To add a new breakpoint, use the “Add New Breakpoint” button at the bottom.
* To delete a breakpoint, look for the delete icon that appears on hover.
* To add a new action, use the + icon on the respective breakpoint you want to add an action to.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9f95d698-ec6b-4852-a04b-abafdb61a371.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

#### Actions [#actions]

Here are the different actions you can trigger with the Screen Resize Event:

* [Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/transition-action.md)
* [Animation Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/animation-action.md)
* [Sound Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/sound-action.md)
* [Particle Control Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/particles-control-action.md)
* [Video Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/video-action.md)
* [Create Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/create-object-action.md)
* [Destroy Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/destroy-object-action.md)
* [Switch Camera Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/switch-camera-action.md)
* [Scene Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/scene-transition-action.md)
* [Reset Scene Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/reset-scene-action.md)
* [Set Variable Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/set-variable-action.md)
* [Variable Control Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/variable-control-action.md)
* [Conditional Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/conditional-action.md)
* [Clear Local Storage Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/clear-local-storage-action.md)
* [API Request Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/api-request-action.md)


# Scroll Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/scroll-event)



The Scroll Event requires the user to scroll in order to go through the transitions added inside.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3266f025-b2f6-498e-8a86-1d43ee052632.mp4" />

There are two types available on the Scroll Event

* **Steps** → The amount of scroll needed to go from the beginning of a transition to the end.
* **Scroll →** lets you transition between states (set up in the actions section) based on the scroll of your web page.

Here are the different Actions you can trigger with the Scroll Event:

* [Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/transition-action.md)
* [Animation Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/animation-action.md)
* [Create Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/create-object-action.md)
* [Set Variable Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/set-variable-action.md)
* [Variable Control Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/variable-control-action.md)
* [Conditional Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/conditional-action.md)
* [Clear Local Storage Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/clear-local-storage-action.md)
* [API Request Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/api-request-action.md)

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/886f2e69-927f-45e3-8b6f-945dc446820e.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

### Type Scroll [#type-scroll]

The **Scroll** event has a Type called **Scroll*&#x2A; which only works with the &#x2A;**Viewer export***.

This type lets you transition between states (set up in the actions section) based on the scroll of your web page.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/31e175ef-6f7a-4e17-be12-26ff45d8b2ae.mp4" />

<Callout>
  *👉 Check out the demo at: [https://viewer-scroll-event.framer.website/](https://viewer-scroll-event.framer.website/)*
</Callout>

#### Start From - Enter View [#start-from---enter-view]

Objects with scroll events will start animating once the viewer component comes into view on your web page.

* **Start At**
  * **Top** → Animations will start once the top of the component gets into view.
  * **Middle** → Animations will start once the middle of the component gets into view.
  * **Bottom** → Animations will start once the bottom of the component gets into view.

* **Offset** - For each Start At option, you can still add an offset to that option so it starts later.

* **End After** - Defines at what scroll amount from the set Start At location (measured in pixels) should the Transition Action reach its final state.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5bda9684-613c-4010-a41d-36e8783dc632.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

#### Start From - Page [#start-from---page]

Objects with scroll events will start animating at the defined Start At the position of your web page, which means the height from the top of the webpage measured in pixels.

* **Start At** - The position on your web page where the scroll events should start animating.
* **End After** - Defines at what scroll amount (measured in pixels from the top of the web page) should the Transition Action reach its final state.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b387d735-e5bd-4511-9ba4-769216514788.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/HRCtQcUt2dS48gy7AYUs8MCP/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Scroll to interact! 👆 The Scroll Event uses scroll to go through the transitions added inside.*

# State Change Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/state-change-event)



The State Change Event allows you to trigger actions based on the state of an object.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/52581247-8251-4a87-887e-ce3cf4e31dce.mp4" />

Here are the different properties of a State Change Event.

* **Object** - The object we want to check if the state has changed.
* **Changes to** - If the object gets to this state, the actions will be triggered.

In this example, if `Rectangle` changes to `State 1`, the actions under ‘Then’ will be triggered.

And if `Rectangle` changes back to any other state, the actions under ‘Else’ will be triggered.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ea86a822-552f-45ab-a7cc-d9fa4f60c42a.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/BEb5KGCREZd6awNWYbibePAn/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *In this simple example, there is a State Change Event on the Pink Cube which triggers a transition on it when the Purple Cube arrives at State 1.*


# Trigger Area Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/trigger-area-event)



A Trigger Area Event will trigger the actions as soon as any object or a specific object gets inside a defined area.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/76cc847a-2243-4ea5-8750-53eecaf4656c.mp4" />

***

### Settings for Trigger Event [#settings-for-trigger-event]

**Toggle Property**

* The Toggle option means that when the event is first triggered, the actions go one way and when it's triggered again the actions come back, creating a toggle effect.

**Objects**

* All → every object will be considered for the Trigger Area detection.
* Custom → choose a specific object to be considered for the Trigger Area detection.

**Zone**

* The type of area. It can be a box or a sphere.

**Transform** (Size, Position & Rotation)

* Define the transform values for the trigger area.

**Helper**

* Show or hide the visualization of the trigger area in the editor.

**Actions**

Here are the different Actions you can trigger with the Trigger Area Event:

* [Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/transition-action.md)
* [Sound Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/sound-action.md)
* [Open Link Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/open-link-action.md)
* [Create Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/create-object-action.md)
* [Destroy Object Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/destroy-object-action.md)
* [Switch Camera Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/switch-camera-action.md)
* [Scene Transition Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/scene-transition-action.md)
* [Reset Scene Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/reset-scene-action.md)

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/fcb872d4-08f3-4246-b5d6-a348091a693c.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

***

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/floatingstonescenesfix-bd6918be45e8faffacb3431eb74ad9b9/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Try out teleporting via the Trigger Area event + Scene Transition action in the example above by walking into one of the teleports.*


# Variable Change Event (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/variable-change-event)



The Variable Change Event allows you to trigger actions when a specific variable changes.

## Variable Change Event Properties [#variable-change-event-properties]

#### Select Variable [#select-variable]

On the first dropdown, select the variable that the event should check for changes.

Once the event detects a change in the variable value, it will trigger the Actions added to it.

The dropdown list will access any variables available on the current file.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ed7dc797-822b-4e7e-b888-4e493679339b.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

The following variable types can be used:

| Variable type              | Defined by                                                                                                                                                   | Usage                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 🤹 Variables               | Number, String, Boolean, Timers, Counter or Random                                                                                                           | Variables can store values that are reusable. For detailed explanation read the [Variables](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables.md) doc. |
| ℹ️ Object Property         | Object’s position, rotation, or scale (X, Y, Z), or it’s width, height and depth as a number, e.g.: `100`                                                    | Capture object property changes, and save them as variables to create advanced interactions.                                                                          |
| 🖱️ Mouse Position (2D)    | The position of the mouse on the screen is defined by X and Y as numbers.                                                                                    | Animate or manipulate objects based on the mouse position                                                                                                             |
| 💥 Mouse Hit Position (3D) | The position of the mouse hitting an object in 3D space, as Position X, Y, or Z of the hit location, or the Position X, Y, and Z of the object that was hit. | Create interactions based on specific hit positions, e.g.: visualize hit in 3D space.                                                                                 |
