# AI Text OpenAI API (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/ai-text-open-ai-api)



The OpenAI API integration in Spline allows you to harness the power of advanced AI models like GPT-3.5 Turbo and GPT-4 Turbo. By connecting to OpenAI, you can generate dynamic content, automate responses, and create interactive experiences based on AI-driven outputs within your Spline projects.

<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/Vvzbjk4Zw2s" title="YouTube video player" />

***

## Creating a New OpenAI API item [#creating-a-new-openai-api-item]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/56a7288a-a29c-424b-84ab-7cd68c6dbc9f.png" alt="image" />

1. Navigate to the **Variables & Data Panel** on the right sidebar of Spline editor (accessible when nothing is selected in editor).
2. Click on the “+” icon in top right corner.
3. Click on **OpenAI API**.

***

## Configuring OpenAI API [#configuring-openai-api]

* **Model Selection**: Choose the AI model that best suits your needs, following models are available out of the box: GPT-3.5 Turbo, GPT-4 Turbo, GPT-4o mini, GPT-4o.
* **API Key**: Enter your OpenAI API key. [Learn how to get your API key](https://platform.openai.com/docs/quickstart).

  > *Note: To use OpenAI’s API, you’ll need to set up a **paid OpenAI account** and choose a **pay-as-you-go pricing plan** or subscribe to one of the **monthly packages**.*
* **Request on Start**: Decide if the API request should automatically start when the scene loads (Yes or No).

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3e1acbfa-b163-436c-bbe2-dcc4ffdadf4d.png" alt="image" maxWidth="600" />

* **Prompt**: Define the prompt that will be sent to the AI model. This prompt will guide the AI in generating the desired output.

  *Note: The prompt supports non-English languages. The latest models, like GPT-4o, have better performance across non-English languages. [Learn more](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/6742369-how-do-i-use-the-openai-api-with-text-in-different-languages)*
* **Behavior**: Toggle the switch on to be able to specify additional behavior properties for the AI model. For example, you might set a persona like “I am a teacher from the 70s” to influence the AI’s responses.

***

## Response handling [#response-handling]

* **Preview**: View the AI-generated response in real-time within the Response tab.
* **Refresh**: Click the refresh button to retrieve an updated response from the AI.

***

## Using OpenAI API in Spline [#using-openai-api-in-spline]

#### API Updated Event [#api-updated-event]

This event triggers when the API receives a response.

[API Updated Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/api-updated-event.md)

#### API Request Action [#api-request-action]

You can trigger an OpenAI API request based on specific events, such as the start of the scene, a user interaction, or any other custom event in your Spline project.

[API Request Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/api-request-action.md)

#### Using Input with OpenAI API [#using-input-with-openai-api]

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/21666a8b-4785-4096-b1a7-902e91d66e43.mp4" />

To enable the users to submit a custom prompt in an input field with Spline directly, create an Input object. Learn more about it here:

[Input](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/concepts/input.md)

#### Using Variables with OpenAI API [#using-variables-with-openai-api]

OpenAI API responses can be mapped to variables within Spline, allowing for dynamic and responsive interactions based on AI-generated content.

* **Set Variables from OpenAI Response**: Map the AI-generated responses to specific variables (e.g., Strings for text responses, Booleans for conditional logic). For example, if the AI returns a response based on user input, you can store this in a **String** variable and display it dynamically within your scene.
* **Update Object Properties**: Attach variables to object properties so that changes in the AI’s output are reflected in real-time. For instance, change the content of a text object to display the AI’s response or adjust the appearance of an object based on the sentiment of the AI’s generated text.
* **Trigger Actions Based on Variables**: Use **Variable Change Events** to trigger specific actions when the OpenAI API updates a variable. For example, if the AI generates a specific command or response, you can trigger an animation or change the scene state accordingly.

***

## Practical Use Cases [#practical-use-cases]

<Callout>
  The OpenAI API integration offers limitless possibilities for creating intelligent, dynamic, and interactive experiences within your Spline projects. Here are some practical examples:
</Callout>

* **Interactive Chatbots**:
  * Create a conversational interface where users can interact with an AI-driven character or assistant.
  * Use the AI’s responses to dynamically update dialogue boxes and trigger actions based on user inputs.
* **Dynamic Content Generation**:
  * Leverage the AI to generate content such as story elements or descriptions.
  * Display this content within your Spline project, creating an ever-changing experience based on AI creativity.
* **Personalized Experiences**:
  * Use the AI to tailor content and interactions based on user preferences or behavior.
  * For instance, an AI-driven art gallery could provide custom descriptions and recommendations based on the user’s interactions with the exhibits.

***

## Best Practices [#best-practices]

* **Testing**: Thoroughly test your OpenAI API setup to ensure that it generates the desired output and integrates seamlessly with your Spline project.
* **Prompt Engineering**: Experiment with different prompts to guide the AI toward generating the most relevant and accurate responses.
* **Warning**: Never share sensitive information, such as your OpenAI API key, publicly. Ensure that this data is securely stored and used only within a protected environment.


# AI Voice Assistant API (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/ai-voice-assistant-api)



The AI Voice Assistant feature enables seamless integration with OpenAI's Realtime API for low-latency conversational experiences, allowing to stream real-time audio responses from AI. When activated, it prompts users engaged with your 3D experience to grant microphone access, enabling them to communicate directly with the AI via voice and receive spoken AI responses.

Paired with the AI conversational functionality, you can influence your 3D scene with AI by using variables like the Voice Spectrum or using the [AI Assistant Trigger Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-trigger-event.md) to have the AI trigger actions defined by you.

<Callout>
  **AI Voice Assistant API** is a paid feature; you’ll need an active **Pro** or **Max** subscription.
</Callout>

## Creating a new AI Voice Assistant API [#creating-a-new-ai-voice-assistant-api]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ed52744b-3d1c-4bad-9558-7d0a0d8de083.png" alt="image" />

1. Navigate to the **Variables & Data Panel** on the right sidebar of the Spline editor (accessible when nothing is selected in the editor).
2. Click on the “+” icon in the top right corner.
3. Click on **AI Voice Assistant**.

## Configuring an AI Voice Assistant [#configuring-an-ai-voice-assistant]

* **API Key**: Enter your OpenAI API key. [Learn how to get your API key](https://platform.openai.com/docs/quickstart#create-and-export-an-api-key).

  > **Note:** To use OpenAI’s API, you’ll need to set up a paid OpenAI developer account (different from the ChatGPT plans). To do so, navigate to the [Billing section](https://platform.openai.com/settings/organization/billing/overview) in your developer account dashboard and set up your billing information. Learn more about [OpenAI API Pricing](https://openai.com/api/pricing/).

* **Voice**: Choose the AI voice.

### Instructions [#instructions]

You can define as many guidelines as you’d like for the AI to follow. By default, a set of instructions will already be provided, but you can either modify or replace them entirely to suit your needs.

This approach unlocks a wide range of possibilities. For instance, you can use a variable change event to trigger specific actions when the AI updates certain variables, allowing for dynamic responses tailored to various contexts.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/7dc839ab-29b7-4e87-ac14-81049d4aa497.png" alt="image" maxWidth="600" />

### Personality [#personality]

In the personality section, you can define the AI’s initial character and influence how it expresses itself and communicates. For example, you could instruct it to be upbeat and genuine, or convey confusion and nervousness, depending on your needs.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1a767572-1348-4cb9-9c57-56e3ad1c5ce5.png" alt="image" maxWidth="600" />

### Events [#events]

In the events section, you can create new AI Assistant Trigger events. Each event includes a description, and when a user request matches that description, the AI will recognize it and execute the associated actions.

Learn more about the [AI Assistant Trigger Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-trigger-event.md).

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/697ad9d7-a1c9-42ed-994f-20737861a485.png" alt="image" maxWidth="600" />

<Callout>
  *Note: All written instructions (Instructions, Personality and Trigger descriptions) support non-English languages. You can also speak with the AI in different languages and will respond in the same language. Only languages supported by OpenAI.*
</Callout>

## AI Voice Spectrum Variable [#ai-voice-spectrum-variable]

The AI Voice Assistant API also includes a voice spectrum variable, which in simple terms means the sound of the voice. This value can be linked to other variables and consequently to object properties to manipulate 3D objects in your scene.

To achieve this, you can use the [AI Assistant Listener Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-listener-event.md), an event that continuously listens for changes in the audio spectrum. When the audio spectrum changes, it updates your variable accordingly.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/e7efa6f4-7543-4c1f-9ca3-8202bb8a04a9.png" alt="image" maxWidth="400" />

### Pairing Voice Spectrum with Morph Targets [#pairing-voice-spectrum-with-morph-targets]

When importing an object with [Morph Targets Support](https://docs.spline.design/importing-content/morph-targets.md), you can assign variables to each target and use the voice spectrum to influence their values. This allows you to simulate effects like characters speaking and other dynamic deformations.

Learn more about related topics:

* [AI Assistant Trigger Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-trigger-event.md)
* [AI Assistant Listener Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/ai-assistant-listener-event.md)
* [Variables](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables.md)


# Real-time API (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/real-time-api)



The Real-time API feature in Spline allows you to integrate external services, retrieve or send data, and automate responses based on events. This enables dynamic and interactive experiences within your Spline projects.

<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/ga6R8qDqLHo" title="YouTube video player" />

***

## Creating a New API item [#creating-a-new-api-item]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/16bb6e8b-1716-4a7d-8dc0-eb05887e47b8.png" alt="image" />

1. Navigate to the **Variables & Data Panel** on the right sidebar of Spline editor (accessible when nothing is selected in editor).
2. Click on the **APIs** tab.
3. Click on **New API** to create a new API item.

## Configuring an API [#configuring-an-api]

Select the way you would like to communicate with the API, from the following options

* **GET**: Retrieve data.
* **POST**: Send data.
* **DELETE**: Remove data.
* **PUT**: Update data.
* **HEAD**: Retrieve headers only.
* **OPTIONS**: Describe allowed methods.
* **PATCH**: Apply partial modifications.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/caf63f02-8e33-42ff-b998-e9ae5a145d47.png" alt="image" maxWidth="500" />

**API URL:** add in the URL of your API

**Request on Start:** Choose whether the API request should automatically start when the scene loads (Yes or No).

**Headers:** Add necessary headers by entering the Key and Value. Use the “+” icon to add multiple headers.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ef5deedf-c406-4e7f-bd08-12532b224614.png" alt="image" maxWidth="600" />

***

## Response handling [#response-handling]

**Preview**: The API response can be previewed directly within the Response tab.

**Refresh**: Click the refresh button to retrieve the latest response from the API.

***

## Using an API response in Spline [#using-an-api-response-in-spline]

#### API Updated Event [#api-updated-event]

This event triggers when the API receives a response. Learn more about:

[API Updated Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/api-updated-event.md)

#### API Request Action [#api-request-action]

You can trigger an API request based on specific events, such as the start of the scene, a mouse click, or any other custom event. Learn more about:

[API Request Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/api-request-action.md)

#### Using Variables with APIs [#using-variables-with-apis]

When using APIs, the data retrieved can be stored in variables, which can then be used to dynamically update properties within your Spline project. Here’s how you can leverage variables:

* **Set Variables from API Response:** You can map the response data to specific variables (e.g., Numbers, Strings, Booleans). For example, if your API returns a temperature value, you can store this value in a **Number** variable.
* **Update Object Properties**: Variables can be attached to object properties such as position, scale, or color. For instance, if you receive a new position from the API, you can update the object’s position dynamically.
* **Trigger Actions Based on Variables**: Use the **Variable Change Event** to trigger actions when the API updates the variables. For example, if an API updates a String variable with new text, you can automatically update a text label in your scene.

***

## Practical Use Cases [#practical-use-cases]

<Callout>
  APIs provide endless possibilities for integrating external services into your Spline projects, enabling you to create highly customized and dynamic experiences. Depending on the provider you choose to integrate with, the functionality can vary widely. Here are some examples:
</Callout>

* **Dynamic Weather Display**:
  * Use a weather API to retrieve real-time weather data.
  * Store the temperature in a Number variable and display it on a text object.
  * Use Variable Change Events to trigger visual changes, such as modifying the transforms of objects in your scene to reflect changes in weather conditions.
* **Live Scoreboard**:
  * Integrate a sports API to fetch live scores.
  * Update the score in real-time using variables and display it on the scoreboard.
  * Trigger animations when a new score is received.
* **General API Integrations**:
  * Connect with services like **Airtable** to manage and display dynamic data in your scene.
  * Use **OpenAI** to generate text dynamically, providing real-time updates based on AI-generated responses.
  * Integrate with any other REST API to fetch or send data, allowing for virtually limitless possibilities in terms of what your Spline project can achieve.

***

## Best Practices [#best-practices]

* **Testing**: Test your API configuration thoroughly to ensure it works as expected.
* **Optimization**: Minimize the number of API calls by using the Request on Start option only when necessary.
* **Warning**: Never share sensitive information, such as API keys or authorization tokens, publicly. Ensure that these details are securely stored and used only within a protected environment.


# Variables (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/variables)



<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/Id_SZVCUVMM" title="YouTube video player" />

*Tutorial: How to use variables to create interactive 3d content in Spline*

Variables allow you to create reusable values/parameters. They can be applied to many object properties (like Position, Scale, Rotation, etc.) and can be combined with events and actions. This enables you to create highly customized interactions that understand logic.

Some high-level concepts for variables are:

* Create variables such as **Numbers**, **Strings**, and **Booleans**
* Use Dynamic variables: **Time**, **Counter**, **Random**
* Attach variables to object properties and text fields.
* Trigger events from variables
* Capture object and mouse property changes

By leveraging variables, along with [Variable Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/variable-change-event.md) and [Set Variable Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/set-variable-action.md), you can craft truly interactive and dynamic experiences. We encourage you to explore, experiment, and push the boundaries of creativity with variables in Spline.

***

## Variables [#variables]

Variables are data containers with values that can change. The following variable types are available:

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/06c71a80-ee47-48f4-a152-baa5e7425036.png" alt="image" maxWidth="500" />

### 🔢 Number [#number]

* Easily manage numerical data to control and animate objects.
* Can be a decimal or a round number, e.g.: `9` or `67,123`
* Usage: alter or adjust any property on your object that uses a number (e.g.: position, rotation, scale, material layer opacity, etc.)

### 🔤 String [#string]

* Can be any text value, e.g.: `some text`
* Incorporate text-based variables for labels, messaging or updating the status of your experience.

### 🔘 Boolean [#boolean]

* Toggle between `true` and `false` states for interactive behavior.
* With the help of booleans, you can build sophisticated logic into your experience, especially if you combine it with [Conditional Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/conditional-action.md) and [Set Variable Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/set-variable-action.md).

***

## Dynamic variables [#dynamic-variables]

Dynamic variables are changing over time based on specific conditions. These unlock the creation of interactive experiences driven by variables. The following dynamic variable types are available: **Time**, **Counter**, &#x2A;*Random.**

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9cf4a1da-aa96-4e4f-8f47-c3cf55534e87.png" alt="image" maxWidth="500" />

### 🕓 Time [#time]

* Harness the flow of time to create dynamic animations and transitions
* It’s defined by a string that can be applied as the content of a Text object.
* It has following types: **Clock**, **Timer**, **Stopwatch**
* All types have unique property options, listed below

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/379a67f1-0790-4996-9e75-8c6bdc3fe13e.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

🕓 **Clock** → Updating time, based on local time or on a specific time zone.

| Property  | Explanation                                                           | Options                                            |
| --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| Format    | Change the appearance of the time based on common formatting options. | `HH:mm:ss` `mm:ss` `Seconds` `Seconds (3-decimal)` |
| 12/24h    | Option to use 24 hours based time.                                    | `12h` `12h AM/PM` `24h`                            |
| Time Zone | Show the local time zone or a specific time zone.                     | `None`, from `UTC -12` to `UTC+14`                 |

⏲️ **Timer** → Calculating from a pre-defined time backwards, e.g.: from 30 seconds to 0 seconds

| Property  | Explanation                                                                        | Options                                                                                                              |
| --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Format    | Change the appearance of the time based on common formatting options.              | `HH:mm:ss` `mm:ss` `Seconds` `Seconds (3-decimal)`                                                                   |
| Value     | At what time should the timer/stopwatch start                                      | `H` – hour, `M` – minute, `S` – second                                                                               |
| End Value | If set to `YES` it can be defined when should the timer end                        | `H` – hour, `M` – minute, `S` – second                                                                               |
| On End    | Can be decided if the timer should restart, or end after it reached the end value. | `Restart` – restarts the timer after reaches the end value, `Stop` – stops the timer after it reaches the end value. |

⏱️ **Stopwatch** → Calculating from a pre-defined time forward, e.g.: from 0 seconds to 30 seconds.

| Property  | Explanation                                                                            | Options                                                                                                                      |
| --------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Format    | Change the appearance of the time based on common formatting options.                  | `HH:mm:ss` `mm:ss` `Seconds` `Seconds (3-decimal)`                                                                           |
| Value     | At what time should the stopwatch start                                                | `H` – hour, `M` – minute, `S` – second                                                                                       |
| End Value | If set to `YES` it can be defined when should the timer end                            | `H` – hour, `M` – minute, `S` – second                                                                                       |
| On End    | Can be decided if the stopwatch should restart, or end after it reached the end value. | `Restart` – restarts the stopwatch after reaches the end value, `Stop` – stops the stopwatch after it reaches the end value. |

### 🔢 Counter [#counter]

The Counter will start counting in seconds with a set interval defined in seconds. It can be great to use it for iterating through dynamic sequences or loops, e.g.: driving an animation with the counter.

| Property  | Explanation                                                                        | Options                                                                                                              |
| --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Format    | Change the appearance of the time based on common formatting options.              | `HH:mm:ss` `mm:ss` `Seconds` `Seconds (3-decimal)`                                                                   |
| Value     | At what time should the counter start                                              | `H` – hour, `M` – minute, `S` – second                                                                               |
| Interval  |                                                                                    |                                                                                                                      |
| Step      |                                                                                    |                                                                                                                      |
| End Value | If set to `YES` it can be defined when should the timer end                        | `H` – hour, `M` – minute, `S` – second                                                                               |
| On End    | Can be decided if the timer should restart, or end after it reached the end value. | `Restart` – restarts the timer after reaches the end value, `Stop` – stops the timer after it reaches the end value. |

### 🔀 Random [#random]

Random will generate a random value within a set range. With random values create randomness and unpredictability in your project, e.g., mini-game logic and procedural visuals.

| Property      | Explanation                                                                                            | Options                               |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------- |
| Default value | A default value can be defined for the variable.                                                       | `number`                              |
| Static        | Random can be static, or non-static                                                                    | `Yes`, `No`                           |
| Interval      | If Static is set to No, a new random number will be generated with the set interval defined in seconds | `S` – interval defined in seconds     |
| Minimum       | The range in which the random numbers should be picked, defining it’s minimum value.                   | `number`                              |
| Maximum       | The range in which the random numbers should be picked, defines it’s maximum value.                    | `number`                              |
| Decimals      | Defining the formatting of the random numbers.                                                         | `0 (Integer)`, `0.0`, `0.00`, `0.000` |

***

## Local Storage [#local-storage]

Enabling local storage on your variables will allow you to store persistent data on your exported scenes using variables.

<Callout>
  **Variables Local Storage** is a paid feature; you’ll need an active **Pro** or **Max** subscription to use it.
</Callout>

Try it on the demo: [→ Bar Chart - Persistent](https://my.spline.design/barchartpersistent-52a67ab58b5afe48ee6f62ca8a40d842/)

### Enabling Local Storage [#enabling-local-storage]

1. Open the variables panel, from the right sidebar.
2. Find the variable you want to make persistent with local storage.
3. Press the local storage icon that shows up on hover.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/2e114ad3-2273-4a21-9cf9-6c7e3dce1e74.png" alt="image" maxWidth="500" />

To reset the persistent data of your variables back to their default values using an event trigger you can use the [Clear Local Storage Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/clear-local-storage-action.md).

***

## Events supporting variables [#events-supporting-variables]

Following Events have access to the [Set Variable Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/set-variable-action.md). This means when any of these events are performed, a variable can be set to a new value via an expression – either another variable or a custom calculation.

* [Variable Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/variable-change-event.md)
* [Start Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/start-event.md)
* [Mouse Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-up-event.md)
* [Mouse Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-down-event.md)
* [Mouse Hover Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-hover-event.md)
* [Key Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-up-event.md)
* [Key Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-down-event.md)
* [Key Press Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-press-event.md)
* [Scroll Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/scroll-event.md)
* [Drag and Drop Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/drag-and-drop-event.md)
* [Distance Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/distance-event.md)
* [State Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/state-change-event.md)
* [Collision Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/collision-event.md)
* [Trigger Area Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/trigger-area-event.md)

Some high-level concepts for using variables with events and actions:

* [Variable Change Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/variable-change-event.md) - A special event that is triggered when a variable’s value changes. Pro tip: use these with the dynamic variables (e.g.: counter) to animate an object’s property.
* [Set Variable Action](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/actions/set-variable-action.md) - An action that allows you to modify variables and object properties. It can be either another referenced variable or can be a custom calculation.

***

## How to use variables [#how-to-use-variables]

Here is a simple guide to creating variables, assigning them to a cube, and updating it via an event.

### Create variables [#create-variables]

1. Open the variable table from the right sidebar, it’s visible when no objects are selected.
2. Select a variable type, for this exercise select “Number”.
3. Change the number to a specific value in the right column, e.g.: `100`.
4. Double-click the name of the variable “Number”, to change its name, name it something like `Initial Position`.
5. Right-click the variable and select “duplicate” to duplicate it.
6. Name the duplicate something like `New Position` and adjust it’s value to `200`.
7. Done, the variables are ready to be used!

### Assign a variable to an object [#assign-a-variable-to-an-object]

1. Create a cube (select it in the top toolbar and click on the viewport).
2. Select the cube.
3. Hover any of it’s properties (e.g.: Position X).
4. Click on the little dot in the upper right corner.
5. Select the variable to be assigned, `Initial Position` in this case.
6. Done, the object is driven by a variable!

### Update variables with events and actions [#update-variables-with-events-and-actions]

Variables can be updated with all sorts of events and actions. Here is a simple example of updating the variable via a mouse click.

1. Select the cube.
2. Add an event in the right sidebar at the events section.
3. Open the event “Start” by clicking on it.
4. Change the event type in the top dropdown to “Mouse Down”.
5. Add an action “Set Variable”.
6. Expand the action by clicking on the “>” icon.
7. Select the variable you would like to change, it should be `Initial Position` in this exercise.
8. In the “To” section there is an “Add expression” field, here is the place where you can define what should be the selected variable updated to when the event is performed. Click in the field and select the initially created `New Position` variable.
9. Enter Play mode and click on the cube.
10. Congrats! You have created your first variable update via a Mouse Down Event.

***

## Best practices and troubleshooting [#best-practices-and-troubleshooting]

* **Keep It Simple**: Start with a few variables and build complexity as you gain confidence.
* **Test and Iterate**: Experiment with different variable values to find the perfect balance for your spline project.
* **Variable Not Updating?**: Ensure that the Variable Change events and Set Variable actions are correctly configured and referencing the right variable. Also, check the formula being used.
* **Unexpected Behavior**: Double-check your logic to identify any discrepancies in your variable usage.


# Webhooks (/interaction-states-events-and-actions/variables-and-data/webhooks)



Webhooks in Spline allow you to create real-time, event-driven interactions by listening for and responding to external events. By using webhooks, your Spline project can automatically trigger actions, update variables, and react to data sent from external sources, enabling dynamic and interactive experiences.

<Embed src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/ga6R8qDqLHo" title="YouTube video player" />

***

## Creating a New Webhook [#creating-a-new-webhook]

1. Navigate to the **Variables & Data Panel** on the right sidebar of Spline editor (accessible when nothing is selected in editor).
2. Select the **Webhooks** tab.
3. Click on the **New Webhook** button to create a new Webhook item.
4. Rename the Webhook, Change the default name ("New Webhook") to something more descriptive to better organize your webhooks.

***

## Configuring Webhooks [#configuring-webhooks]

* **Webhook URL**: A default webhook URL will be automatically generated. Copy this URL to integrate with external services that support Webhooks. This URL will listen for incoming data
* **Configure Parameters**: Predefine the variables your webhook will receive by clicking on the “+” icon. The supported types include Strings, Numbers, and Booleans.
* **The example code snippet** will automatically adjust based on the variables you set. You can edit the snippet to use any value you want. The snippet is just an example using Curl, but you can also use any automation tool, such as Zapier, to call the webhook.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/ec2b883d-f2e4-4242-b0fe-b7af961fa3f9.png" alt="image" maxWidth="600" />

***

## Using Webhooks in Spline [#using-webhooks-in-spline]

Webhooks are powerful tools that can trigger actions and update variables in real-time based on external events. Here’s how to utilize them effectively:

#### Webhook Called Event [#webhook-called-event]

This event is triggered whenever your webhook receives data from an external source.

* **Select Webhook**: Assign the specific webhook you want to monitor for incoming data.
* **Actions**: Define the actions to execute when the webhook is called. Actions could include updating variables, triggering animations, or initiating other API calls.

[Webhook Called Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/webhook-called-event.md)

#### Using Variables with Webhooks [#using-variables-with-webhooks]

Webhook data can be mapped to variables, allowing you to dynamically update and control elements within your Spline project based on real-time data.

* **Set Variables from Webhook Data**: Map incoming webhook data to Spline variables (e.g., Numbers, Strings, Booleans). For example, if your webhook sends a temperature reading, store this value in a **Number** variable to display it in your project.
* **Update Object Properties:** Attach variables to object properties so that changes in webhook data are immediately reflected in your scene. For instance, update a text field with the latest information from the webhook or change an object’s color based on the data received.
* **Trigger Actions Based on Variables**: Use **Variable Change Events** to trigger specific actions when a webhook updates a variable. For example, when a Boolean variable (e.g., "Alert Status") is set to `true`, you could trigger an animation, alert, or change the state of an object in your scene.

***

## Practical Use Cases [#practical-use-cases]

<Callout>
  Webhooks offer endless possibilities for integrating real-time data and external events into your Spline projects. Here are some examples of how webhooks can be used:
</Callout>

* **Real-time Notifications**:
  * Use webhooks to receive real-time notifications from services like Slack, Zapier, or other custom systems.
  * Display these notifications directly within your Spline project or trigger animations based on the incoming data.
* **IoT Integration**:
  * Connect your Spline project to IoT devices via webhooks.
  * For example, update the state of objects in your scene based on data from a smart home device, such as a temperature sensor or motion detector.
* **Custom Alerts and Responses**:
  * Set up webhooks to listen for specific external events, such as changes in a database or new messages from an API, and trigger responses in your project.
  * For instance, if a webhook detects that a certain threshold has been met (like a stock price or environmental sensor reading), you can trigger a visual or audio alert in your Spline scene.

***

## Best Practices [#best-practices]

* **Test Your Webhook**: Ensure that your webhook is correctly configured and responding as expected before deploying it in a live environment. Testing helps you verify that the data is being received and processed correctly.
* **Security**: Always use secure, encrypted connections (HTTPS) for your webhook URLs. Avoid exposing sensitive information, such as authorization tokens, publicly, and ensure they are securely stored and transmitted.
* **Debugging**: Use logging and debugging tools to monitor incoming webhook data. This can help you troubleshoot issues and ensure that your actions and variable updates are functioning as intended.


# Creating Material Layers (/materials-shading/basics/creating-material-layers)

> How material layers stack, and how to add, reorder and combine them to build a surface.



<Callout>
  To create a new material layer, select an object, go to the Material panel in the Right Sidebar, then press "+" to add a new layer.
</Callout>

On Spline, materials work in a layer-based approach, which means that you can control an object's look by creating or editing layers on a vertical stack. This approach is different from traditional 3d editors, but it is akin to how most graphic design tools work.

## The default material layers [#the-default-material-layers]

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/c1724073-42bd-4a29-b43d-5169711813e9.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

By default, any object on Spline includes two material layers: Color and Lighting.

For 2D objects like the Rectangle and the Circle, the Lighting layer is set to "None", which means that it doesn't have any visual effect on the object. For 3D objects, the Lighting layer is set to “Phong” by default.

## Layer Properties [#layer-properties]

All material layers are different, but they all share the Opacity and Blending parameters.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5748478d-e795-4329-81c1-687fe3317d6d.mp4" />


# Layer Masking (/materials-shading/basics/layer-masking)



Layer masking allows selective control of the visibility or transparency of specific areas within a material. It involves using greyscale to determine which parts of the masked layer are visible or hidden. Closer to white will be more visible and closer to black will be more hidden.

This technique is helpful for texturing, compositing, and creating complex visual effects to texture your 3D objects.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1f1b8a0b-d721-4d08-863b-86e560c06eda.mp4" />

The following layers support Mask Mode:

* [Image Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/image-layer.md)
* [Noise Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/noise-layer.md)
* [Pattern Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/pattern-layer.md)
* [Metcap Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/matcap-layer.md)
* [Video Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/video-layer.md)
* [Depth Layer / 3D Gradient](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/depth-layer-3d-gradient.md)
* [Gradient Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/gradient-layer.md)
* [Fresnel Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/fresnel-layer.md)
* [Rainbow Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/rainbow-layer.md)

## How to create a Layer Mask [#how-to-create-a-layer-mask]

To create a layer mask, you’ll need to:

1. Select a layer that supports Mask Mode or add a new one on the Material Panel.
2. Open the layer settings by clicking the layer thumbnail and select Mask Mode.
3. Add the layer you want to mask right below the Mask layer.

<Callout>
  Remember to use greyscale on your mask. White where you want your masked layer to be visible and black where you want it to be hidden.
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/136d4ece-c02c-4936-9ff0-a0df67cc2f9e.mp4" />

When a layer is in Mask Mode, it becomes purple and has an arrow pointing to the layer below (the layer that is masked).

The Mask will only mask one layer, which is right below it.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/54d7aec5-c826-47e8-b283-22c9098568e3.png" alt="image" maxWidth="400" />


# Material, Color, and Image Assets (/materials-shading/basics/material-color-and-image-assets)

> Save materials, colours and images as reusable assets you can apply across a scene.



**You can create assets for the following types:**

* Material (combination of material layers)
* Color
* Image
* Media (Videos)
* Audio

Created assets can be quickly applied to multiple objects. If the asset is adjusted, all objects using it get updated with the changes.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/bb493e48-4ba1-4720-bf37-ca710b11e27d.mp4" />

When no object is selected, you can find a list of all the assets (Material, Color, Image) created on each scene on the right sidebar.

Clicking on the assets is also a quick way to edit any asset without having to select an object.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/b71a2c51-6599-4642-b99d-3933fd2d9e17.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

## Cleaning up unused assets [#cleaning-up-unused-assets]

It might come in handy to do a clean-up on the unused material assets in your scene to get more organized.

To do so, follow these steps:

1. Unselect everything in your scene.
2. Scroll down on the inspector sidebar until you find the Material Assets Panel.
3. On this panel, click the ‘more’ icon and select ‘Remove Unused Assets’.

This will clean up any assets that are not applied to an object.

Make sure you don’t want to keep any of them but you can undo this action to double-check.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d5d88b1e-5500-4c91-a762-9afd4c0efb06.png" alt="image" />

# PBR Materials (/materials-shading/basics/pbr-materials)

> Physically based materials in Spline — roughness, metalness and texture maps, and how to bring a PBR material set in from another tool.



**PBR** — physically based rendering — is the shading model most 3D tools share.
Instead of describing a surface with ad-hoc highlight settings, you describe what
it's *made of*: how rough it is, whether it's metal, and which texture maps carry
that detail per-pixel. The renderer works out how light should behave.

In Spline this lives in the [Lighting Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/lighting-layer.md)
of a material. Set that layer's mode to **Physical** and you're working with a
PBR surface.

<Callout type="idea">
  PBR materials need something to reflect. A metal in a scene with no environment
  has nothing to pick up and reads as flat grey — pair these materials with an
  [environment or HDRi](https://docs.spline.design/lighting/environment-and-hdri.md).
</Callout>

***

## The two dials that matter most [#the-two-dials-that-matter-most]

| Control       | What it does                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Roughness** | How scattered reflections are. Low is a mirror or polished metal; high is chalk, rubber or unfinished wood                                                 |
| **Metalness** | Whether the surface behaves like metal. Metals take their color from what they reflect; non-metals keep their own color and reflect the environment on top |

Most real-world looks come from these two alone. **Reflectivity** tunes how
strongly a non-metal surface reflects — or use **IOR** (index of refraction) with
**Specular** for the physically-stated version of the same thing, which is what
glass, water and gemstone values are usually quoted as.

***

## Texture maps [#texture-maps]

Any of the dials above can be driven per-pixel by an image instead of a single
value. That's what a downloaded or authored material set gives you:

| Map              | What it carries                                                                                                                      |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Rough Map**    | Per-pixel roughness — worn patches, polished edges, smudges                                                                          |
| **Metal Map**    | Which parts of the surface are metal                                                                                                 |
| **Normal Map**   | Surface detail as direction data, for dents and grain that catch light without extra geometry. **Normal Scale** adjusts the strength |
| **Bump Map**     | The simpler height-based alternative to a normal map                                                                                 |
| **AO Map**       | Baked ambient occlusion — contact shadows in crevices. **AO Intensity** dials it back                                                |
| **Emissive Map** | Which parts glow, with **Emissive** for the color and **Emissive Power** for the strength. Push it past 1 and it blooms              |

### Packed textures [#packed-textures]

Material sets often pack three maps into one image — occlusion in red, roughness
in green, metalness in blue. Each map has a **Channel** selector (Luminance, Red,
Green, Blue, Alpha) so you can point all three at the same image and read a
different channel for each, with no need to split it into separate files first.

***

## Beyond the basics [#beyond-the-basics]

Some surfaces need more than roughness and metalness, and the Physical mode has
dedicated lobes for them:

* **Clearcoat** and **Coat Rough** — a thin glossy layer over the base, like car
  paint, lacquered wood or a screen behind glass.
* **Sheen** and **Sheen Rough** — the soft rim of light on fabric: velvet,
  satin, brushed cloth.
* **Iridescence** — color that shifts with viewing angle, like a soap bubble,
  beetle shell or oil slick.

Each stays inactive until you touch it, so a material only carries what it
actually uses.

<Callout>
  PBR texture maps and the extended lobes above render on the **WebGPU** pipeline.
  Set your scene's **Renderer** accordingly before publishing — see
  [Play Settings](https://docs.spline.design/exporting-your-scene/play-settings.md).
</Callout>

***

## Bringing materials in from other tools [#bringing-materials-in-from-other-tools]

If you import a **glTF or GLB** whose materials are already metallic-roughness —
what Blender, Substance, Houdini and most asset libraries export — Spline
recognizes it and wires the maps up for you, packed channels included. You land
on a Physical lighting layer with the base color, roughness, metalness, normal
and occlusion already connected.

[How to Import Content](https://docs.spline.design/importing-content/how-to-import-content.md),
[CAD to Spline: optimization best practices](https://docs.spline.design/importing-content/cad-to-spline-optimization-best-practices.md)

***

## Where to go next [#where-to-go-next]

[Creating Material Layers](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/basics/creating-material-layers.md),
[Lighting Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/lighting-layer.md),
[Bump Map & Roughness Map](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/surface-detail/bump-map-roughness-map.md),
[Reflection Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/reflection-layer.md),
[Environment & HDRi](https://docs.spline.design/lighting/environment-and-hdri.md),
[AI Textures](https://docs.spline.design/spline-ai/ai-textures.md)


# Color Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/color-layer)



The Color Layer is very straight forward and familiar from other design tools.

You can choose a color from the HUE or change the opacity/alpha.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d8a7a5e2-2242-4249-ac83-97bfbf3923f4.mp4" />


# Gradient Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/gradient-layer)



Gradient color shows a transition between 2 or more colors. Following parameters can be adjusted:

* **Type** (Linear, Radial, Polar)
* **Blending** (Linear, Smooth)
* **Ramp** (Adjust transition location between colors. Hint: click to add color to the ramp, select it and hit delete to remove it.)
* **Color** (Adjust for each color on ramp.)
* **Angle** (Define the angle of the gradient.)
* **Offset** (Offset the gradient on the X and Y axis.)
* **Morph** (Create cool morphing effects on the X and Y axis.)

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/f478d20b-f724-4fa7-aa33-0b3b2b4ac0b1.mp4" />


# Image Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/image-layer)



The Image layer allows you to upload, or generate with AI, an image to texture your 3D objects.

## How to create an Image Layer [#how-to-create-an-image-layer]

To add an image layer, you’ll need to:

1. Select an object and add a new layer on the Material Panel.
2. Change the layer to &#x2A;*“Image”** by clicking on the dropdown on the newly created layer.
3. Open the **Image Layer** settings by clicking on the layer thumbnail.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/4422b0e6-c8bd-47be-8141-734be8efaed7.png" alt="image" />

## Image Layer Settings [#image-layer-settings]

You can either upload an image or generate a texture with AI to get started.

### Mode [#mode]

Some layers, including Image Layer, can be used as masks, which you can do by selecting Mask on the layer mode. Learn more here: [Layer Masking](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/basics/layer-masking.md).

### Settings [#settings]

#### Projection [#projection]

Determines how the texture fits onto the model's surface, like adjusting a sticker on a curved or flat area.

* **UV** - Maps textures based on the model's UV coordinates.
* **Planar** - Projects the texture onto the model from a single direction.
* **Spherical** - Wraps the texture around the model as if it were a globe, ideal for round objects.
* **Cylindrical** - Applies the texture around the model like a label on a can, perfect for cylinder-shaped objects.
* **Triplanar** - Projects the texture from three directions and blends them, great for complex surfaces without clear orientation.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/4b89527a-8c79-4505-b35b-36aa7b3e96df.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

#### Wrapping [#wrapping]

Decides how textures behave at edges, whether they repeat, stretch, or cut off.

* **Clamp** - Extends the edges of the texture to the bounds of the surface, preventing tiling but possibly stretching the edge pixels.
* **Repeat** - Tiles the texture across the surface, repeating it both horizontally and vertically.
* **Mirror** - Similar to repeat, but each tile is mirrored, creating a symmetrical tiling effect.

**Sharpness** - Adjusts texture clarity, making it crisp or smooth or pixelated.

**Crop** - Use the texture only on a part of the object without it being stretched.

**Scale** - Changes the texture's size on the model.

**Offset** - Moves the texture's position on the surface of the model.

**Rotation** - Spins the texture around a point, altering its angle on the model.

## Image Assets [#image-assets]

You can save your images as assets to reuse on other image layers.

To save an image asset, you’ll need to:

1. Select the object using the image you want to save.
2. Open the Image Layer panel.
3. On the Image Assets panel, press the + button on the top left, and your image asset will be saved

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8d1892ed-7a33-4f8c-b64d-1dfbe1ed4017.mp4" />

## Bump and Roughness Maps [#bump-and-roughness-maps]

The image layers added to your material can be used as bump and roughness maps.

[Bump Map & Roughness Map](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/surface-detail/bump-map-roughness-map.md)


# Noise Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/noise-layer)



The Noise Layer is ideal to simulate organic color changes in surfaces.

Below are the supported types for noises:

* Simplex
* Simplex Fractal
* Ashima
* Fbm
* Perlin
* Voronoi

You can change size, scale and movement of the noise. Apply multiple colors to create unique variations of noise.

# Pattern Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/pattern-layer)



The Pattern layer can be used to generate and achieve different types of patterns to texture your 3D objects.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/486a5b4c-a257-47d2-a115-107cab57587f.mp4" />

## How to create a Pattern Layer [#how-to-create-a-pattern-layer]

To add a pattern layer, you’ll need to:

1. Select an object and add a new layer on the Material Panel.
2. Change the layer to &#x2A;*“Pattern”** by clicking on the dropdown on the newly created layer.
3. Open the **Pattern Layer** settings by clicking on the layer thumbnail.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5556f448-9e00-4e20-a309-1ee258e5b709.mp4" />

## Pattern Layer Settings [#pattern-layer-settings]

### Mode [#mode]

Some layers, including Pattern Layer, have the ability to be used as masks, which you can do by selecting Mask on the layer mode. Learn more here: [Layer Masking](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/basics/layer-masking.md).

### Settings [#settings]

**Style** → The type of pattern (Circle, Ring, Polygon, Cross, Diamond, Checkerboard, Line, and Wave)

**Parameters for each style** → Each style has its own settings that you can adjust. We encourage you to experiment to find your desired pattern.

Some of these common parameters of the Pattern styles are:

* Projection (UV, Spherical, Triplanar
* Offset (X, Y)
* Colors
* Frequency / Size / Variation / Rotation, etc…
* Vertical (Top, Bottom) /Horizontal (Left, Right)

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/8fcb39c5-3a8e-4088-8008-6bb69a5c352b.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Callout>
  *Pattern styles in action: Circle, Ring, Polygon, Cross, Diamond, Checkerboard, Line, and Wave.*
</Callout>

## Pattern Combinations [#pattern-combinations]

By stacking multiple Pattern Layers you can achieve a lot of different combinations and create truly unique patterns.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/87956a0b-91f1-4fa7-91d9-5d9ef603605c.mp4" />


# Rainbow Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/rainbow-layer)



The Rainbow Layer creates iridescence effects that simulate those of rainbows or bubbles. This is helpful to create surfaces that gradually change color depending on the view angle or illumination changes.

Under Material Panel, you can adjust Frequency, Movement, Color on RGB channels, amount and scale of Noise, as well as the offset on X, Y, and Z axes.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/5cfd19d5-d258-43a4-9959-4034147e3c3b.mp4" />


# Vertex Color Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/vertex-color-layer)

> Show the colours already baked into an imported model's mesh.



The **Vertex Color Layer** displays colour data stored in the mesh itself, rather
than colour you set in Spline.

Many models carry a colour per vertex — sculpts painted in ZBrush or Blender,
photogrammetry scans, point-cloud captures, or exports from CAD and generative
tools. Without this layer that data is ignored and the object renders in a single
flat colour.

***

## Adding the layer [#adding-the-layer]

Select the imported object, then in the **Material*&#x2A; section of the inspector click
&#x2A;*+** and choose **Vertex Color**.

There is nothing to configure — the layer shows what the mesh already contains.

***

## When it does nothing [#when-it-does-nothing]

If the object doesn't change, its mesh has no vertex colour data. That's common:

* **Primitives created in Spline** have no vertex colours — use a
  [Color Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/color-layer.md) or
  [Gradient Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/gradient-layer.md) instead.
* **Some formats and exports drop it.** If your model looked coloured in the tool
  you made it in, check that vertex colours were enabled on export.
* **Textured models** usually carry their colour in an image rather than in the
  mesh — use an [Image Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/color-and-texture/image-layer.md) for those.

See [How to Import Content](https://docs.spline.design/importing-content/how-to-import-content.md) for supported
formats.

***

## Tips [#tips]

* **Stack it.** Add a [Lighting Layer](https://docs.spline.design/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/lighting-layer.md) above it so
  the baked colours still respond to your scene's lights instead of looking flat.
* **Scans:** vertex colours are how most photogrammetry and point-cloud captures
  carry their appearance, so this is usually the first layer to add to one.
* The layer costs almost nothing to render — it's reading data the mesh already
  carries.


# Video Layer (/materials-shading/color-and-texture/video-layer)



The video layer allows you to upload videos and use them as a video texture on 2D and 3D objects.

<Callout>
  Uploading videos is a paid feature, you’ll need to have an active **Starter, Professional** or **Team** subscription to use it.
</Callout>

## How to create a Video Layer [#how-to-create-a-video-layer]

To add a video layer, you’ll need to:

1. Select an object and add a new layer on the Material Panel.
2. Change the layer to &#x2A;*“Video”** by clicking on the dropdown on the newly created layer.
3. Open the **Video Layer** settings by clicking on the thumbnail.
4. Click on the &#x2A;*“Upload Video”** to upload a new video.

You can also save your Video Layers as a video asset to reuse in other objects.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/33059cc1-b822-4170-9582-e1b74fb421c2.png" alt="image" />

## How to Play, Pause, or Stop a Video Layer [#how-to-play-pause-or-stop-a-video-layer]

When adding a new Video Layer, a Start Event is automatically created with a Video Action, making your video autoplay on scene load.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/977a893f-8d3a-46f3-8bbf-acc6eeeb9377.png" alt="image" />

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/120d0c96-ab77-48c4-a74d-0d98884a4a51.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

You can change the event to another type, here’s a list of events where the video action is supported:

* [Start Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/start-event.md)
* [Mouse Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-up-event.md)
* [Mouse Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/mouse-down-event.md)
* [Key Up Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-up-event.md)
* [Key Down Event](https://docs.spline.design/interaction-states-events-and-actions/events/key-down-event.md)

With the Video Action, you can control if you want to Play, Pause or Stop a video layer.

As well as other properties like video Volume, Delay and Loop.

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/iphone14provideo-f43255dbccafd395e683fd56d508fb0c/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Video Layer in action! ☝️ Drag to Orbit*


# Depth Layer / 3D Gradient (/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/depth-layer-3d-gradient)



The Depth Layer (3D Gradient) lets you create gradients in the 3D space. You can control the stops of the gradient and edit the gradient from the viewport itself.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/2efbb107-9c82-4bae-ac13-551caf4417cc.mp4" />

You can simulate light effects, create fake lighting and shadows, create variations of colors, or even simulate light dispersion effects, like you’d see with the light of a candle (also called Sub-Surface Scattering).

**Depth Origin can be set to:**

* Vector → depth defined in the world space.
* Camera → depth is influenced by the camera distance and angle.

**Blending can be se to:**

* Linear → for a linear transition between the set colors.
* Smooth → for a smoother transition between the set colors.

## Adding and adjusting gradient stops [#adding-and-adjusting-gradient-stops]

To add a new gradient stop, you can do so from the right sidebar by clicking anywhere on the ramp or by clicking between any of the already existing points on the viewport controls.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/dd74913a-839e-4f1a-adc2-330440dc6656.mp4" />

### Removing Gradient Stops [#removing-gradient-stops]

To remove one of the stops, click and drag down until you see it outlined in red and the cursor change. Alternatively click to select it and hit delete on your keyboard.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/a53ba7f0-82d4-4280-a1ff-32c5121bd483.mp4" />


# Fresnel Layer (/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/fresnel-layer)



<Callout>
  The Fresnel Layer simulates how we perceive reflections on surfaces depending on angle of the light.
</Callout>

The Fresnel Layer shows a color that fades away as it gets closer to being parallel to the angle of the camera (which in real life can be the eye).

The effect is handy to reproduce shiny or metallic surfaces.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/167cd642-0dd0-4feb-8a5a-d59dd0f1f9e6.mp4" />


# Glass Layer (/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/glass-layer)



The Glass Layer simulates the appearance and properties of real-world glass.

It mimics how glass interacts with light, by giving objects properties like transparency, reflectivity, refraction, and blur.

## How to create a Glass Layer [#how-to-create-a-glass-layer]

To add a glass layer, you’ll need to:

1. Select an object and add a new layer on the Material Panel.
2. Change the layer to &#x2A;*“Glass”** by clicking on the dropdown on the newly created layer.
3. Open the **Glass Layer** settings by clicking on the layer thumbnail.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/decfc45c-ea31-4e6e-b632-7beaaec9b0ea.mp4" />

## Glass Layer Settings [#glass-layer-settings]

Control the settings of a glass layer to achieve your desired effect. Additionally, you can combine your glass layer with other layers like fresnel, matcap, or noise to enhance your material further.

### Blur [#blur]

Makes objects seen through the glass look blurred, like looking through frosted glass.

### Thickness [#thickness]

Decides how chunky the glass looks and affects how it bends light and distorts views.

### Refraction [#refraction]

Bends light passing through the glass, changing how clear or distorted objects behind it appear.

<Image src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/775866f0-9d49-44c2-be85-759e2012983b.png" alt="image" maxWidth="300" />

<Embed src="https://my.spline.design/JZ54zFo6wFR6Rmjvnm1tTast/" title="Spline Embed" height="384" />

> *Check out the glass layer in action in this scene. 👆 Press to orbit*


# Lighting Layer (/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/lighting-layer)



The Lighting Layer lets you select from 4 different types of lighting for your objects:

* **Lambert**: Matte, without shining highlights (Concrete, Sand).
* **Phong**: Adds specular reflection (Plastic, Skin).
* **Physical**: Similar to Phong, but with more controls (Metal, Ceramic).
* **Toon**: Cartoonish effect (Non Photorealistic look).

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/9863f10e-7639-4b57-b3f9-9b2ff80363fd.mp4" />


# Matcap Layer (/materials-shading/shading-and-reflection/matcap-layer)



A Matcap layer (Material Capture) works by projecting a texture on the geometry. This is useful to simulate complex material surfaces on your models. By uploading a Matcap or selecting one from the Spline Library, you can quickly add shine, reflections, and color to your objects.

Matcaps function independently of the scene’s lighting and perspective, creating a consistent material appearance on the 3D object. This technique is similar to applying a texture that makes the object look like it’s made from specific materials, such as metal, plastic, or wood.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/3a25c381-4f8e-4d35-8747-8b7235bef4c6.mp4" />

## How to create a Matcap Layer [#how-to-create-a-matcap-layer]

To add a Matcap layer, you’ll need to:

1. Select the object and click on the `+` to add a new layer on the Material panel.
2. Change the layer to “**Matcap**” by clicking on the dropdown on the newly created layer.
3. Open the **Matcap Layer** settings by clicking on the layer thumbnail.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/6b0dcfbf-a483-48b5-bd39-4abd02f97537.mp4" />

## Settings of Matcap Layer [#settings-of-matcap-layer]

### Change Matcap image [#change-matcap-image]

You can select the available Matcaps within the app or upload custom matcaps by clicking on the Matcap layer thumbnail.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/1f728efc-da69-4a10-9dbd-e21d031bdbdd.mp4" />

### Matcap Rotation [#matcap-rotation]

You can adjust the rotation of your Matcap. This property is animatable, allowing you to adjust the rotation between different states to create an animation.

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/e31cd4e1-2dcd-4fb7-8cba-bbc622fd825e.mp4" />

<Callout>
  You can adjust the Matcap blending mode so that it affects other materials or textures applied to the same object 👇
</Callout>

<Video src="https://cdn.spline.design/_assets/docs/d0973a09-c5a7-417a-af89-689687c75880.mp4" />

[Balance Token by @machingtse](https://app.spline.design/community/file/c3223c4b-19f8-4b7c-974d-d5e2407c9122)

Check these tutorials to learn how to apply Matcaps to your designs.
